(batched Qs, each with a keep/move/remove/fix rec AND a counter-argument) -> ACT (worktree
slices, verifier gate, 5-phase fixture audit before commit). Every ruling recorded here.

## Operator's four asks (do not narrow)

1. Colour is optically off + panels do not cohere - explore a THEME SWAP, not cell patches.

2. 2-3 LAYOUT ALTERNATIVES per page to choose between, not one proposal to approve.
3. Content audit: some daily-read data points redundant, others missing.
4. Earlier per-page reviews never finished; E11 sweep INCOMPLETE.

## 2026-11-04 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 106 (RM-1365)

### RULING
- **Escape belongs to what the operator can see.** The one Escape dispatcher (`web/js/lib/escape_layers.js`) skips an open layer whose element has no box (`getClientRects()` empty), the same rendered test `main.js` `_letterShortcutsYield` and `review_player.js` `_menuOpen` already apply to a menu left open by a view change. Measured before the fix, headless Chromium 1920x920: the Lobby mode menu or lane popup opened from the keyboard and stranded by a hash change or Back took the first Escape on Replay unseen, so zen stayed on. Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_escape_unrendered_layer.py`, plus `openEntries` in `escape_layers.test.mjs`.
- A visibility:hidden or opacity 0 layer still has a box and still takes the press; a grep of `web/css` finds no layer hidden that way (not measured in a browser).
- The stranded layer keeps its open class; what it shows when the operator returns to Lobby is a separate row.

## 2026-11-04 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 107 (RM-1367)

### RULING
- **A view change never leaves the keyboard on a control nobody can see.** `applyView` (`web/js/main.js`) blurs focus that the change left on an element with no box (`getClientRects()` empty), in the same task as the change. It only blurs and never moves focus anywhere, so a rendered control keeps it (the header view trigger after a keyboard pick from the view menu), and activation code that focuses a control on the new view (the Home Find Match pick to `#lv-mode-trigger`) still runs after it. Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_view_change_stranded_focus.py`.
- Measured before the fix, headless Chromium 1920x920 under `?ui_mock=1`: Chromium's own focus fixup does drop the hidden focus, but only at the next rendering frame. A `setTimeout` 0 after the hash change still read `lv-mode-trigger` with 0 rects; one `requestAnimationFrame` later it read `BODY`. Inside that window an Enter clicked the hidden Lobby trigger once and opened its mode menu on Replay. The row's first report (focus "stays" on the trigger) was a read taken inside that window; a two-frame read passes on the unfixed code, so the guard reads focus inside the hashchange dispatch itself.
- The Enter consequence test rides the frame race (red in 2 of 3 runs on the unfixed code); the hashchange-dispatch read is the deterministic pin.

## 2026-11-04 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 108 (RM-1368)

### RULING
- **A view change closes every transient layer it leaves with no box.** `applyView` (`web/js/main.js`) calls `dismissStrandedLayers()` (`web/js/lib/escape_layers.js`) in the same task as the `body[data-view]` flip, before the RM-1367 focus drop. It dismisses each Escape-registered layer that is open and whose element has no box (`getClientRects()` empty), through the layer's optional `dismiss()` (a closer that never moves focus), else its `close()`. A layer still rendered after the change (the header view menu, the task modal) is left alone, and so is a layer whose `el()` gives no element. Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_view_change_closes_layers.py`, plus `strandedLayers` in `escape_layers.test.mjs`.
- Measured before the fix, headless Chromium 1920x920 under `?ui_mock=1`, each layer opened and its view left by a hash change: the Home Find Match picker (inside `#home-overlay`) kept its open state with 0 rects on Replay, but its scrim `#home-find-match-backdrop` (created on `<body>`, position:fixed, z 9100) stayed over the whole Replay view: `elementFromPoint` at the centre read the backdrop, a click on the rendered `#view-trigger` timed out behind it, and Escape could not clear it (an unrendered layer takes no press, RM-1365). Back on Home the picker was still open. The Lobby mode menu and lane popup kept their open class and `aria-expanded="true"` on Replay and were still open on return to Lobby. This settles the s144 u106 verifier dispute: the picker has no box, its scrim does.
- The RM-1365 dispatcher filter and the RM-1323 letter-shortcut yield stay: they are still the rule for any other way a layer loses its box. Their guards (`test_escape_unrendered_layer.py`, `test_hotkey_letter_owners.py`) built the stranded state from the view change; they now assert the change closed the layer, then put the open class back on the unrendered layer so both filters stay pinned end to end.

## 2026-11-04 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 109 (RM-1366)

### RULING
- **The open menu owns a plain Space on every view, not only on Replay.** `web/js/main.js` `_spaceHeldBehindMenu` prevents a plain or Shift+Space when a `role="menu"` is rendered (`getClientRects()` non-empty, the RM-1323 test) and focus is on nothing a Space activates or types into, and does nothing else with it: the menu stays open, Escape or a click closes it as before. It reuses `isTypingTarget` / `isSpaceActivated` from `review_player_model.js`, so the page hold and the player's RM-1324 hold read focus the same way. Ctrl / Alt / Meta + Space pass (the player's play chord). Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_space_behind_a_menu_any_view.py`.
- Measured before the fix, headless Chromium 1920x920 under `?ui_mock=1`: view menu open, right-click a bare spot of the view (no click fires, so the menu stays open, focus on the body), then Space scrolled the scroller under that spot, unprevented, on six views: Settings 718 px, Session 716, Replay with a match loaded and no recording 253, Champ Select 139, Historical PGR 55 to 65, Build Insights 31. Home, Lobby, Active Match, Post Game Review, History and User Builds had no scroller to move under `ui_mock`. The row (filed as Replay-only, the player stands down with no recording) was one layer too narrow: the player is not on this route at all.
- Alternative not taken: close the menu on a right-click outside it. It changes the pointer contract of four menus to fix a key, and the RM-1323 / RM-1324 rulings already say keys behind an open menu stand down; the hold keeps one rule.
- Each scroll test has a control that serves `main.js` without the hold and must see the same route scroll; the RM-1324 control in `test_replay_keys_behind_menu.py` now strips both holds.

## 2026-11-04 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 110 (RM-1364)

### RULING
- **An open, rendered modal owns the page's shortcuts, as a menu does.** The two full-page scrims, the task detail modal (`#task-modal`) and the cross-session digest popout (`#digest-popout`), now register with `modal: true` in `web/js/lib/escape_layers.js`; `modalLayerRendered()` answers whether such a layer is open with a box (the RM-1365 `openEntries` rule). `web/js/main.js` `_letterShortcutsYield` (A, F, Z, H, ?, Shift+R) and the Replay player's `hotkeyAction` (`modalOpen`: ], [, d, D, Space) both read it.
- Measured before the fix, headless Chromium 1920x920 under `?ui_mock=1`, focus on the body, on Home, Settings, Replay, Lobby and Session: A clicked ANALYZE NOW and Z flipped zen behind either modal; Shift+R wiped preferences and reloaded the page out from under the modal; on Replay `]` seeked a showing recording 20 -> 30 behind the task modal.
- Space behind a modal is NOT held, unlike behind a menu: with focus on the body it scrolled nothing on those five views, and a click inside the modal may want Space to scroll the modal's own content. It no longer plays. A menu painted over a modal is checked first, so its hold still applies.
- Kept: Ctrl / Alt + Space still plays behind a modal (the play-from-anywhere chord, above the check as RM-1324 kept it for a menu); Escape still closes the modal through the dispatcher; a modal open by class with no box (the overlay shell's `display: none !important`) kills no key.
- Not changed, unmeasured: Ctrl+K (focus the prompt) behind a modal; under `?ui_mock=1` it moved focus to nothing measurable. Filed as a follow-up, not ruled.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_shortcuts_behind_a_modal.py` (red 33 failed / 15 passed, green 48 passed), `modalShowing` in `escape_layers.test.mjs`, the `modalOpen` case in `review_player_model.test.mjs`.

## 2026-11-04 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 111 (RM-1424)

### RULING
- **An open modal holds the keyboard focus, and closing it hands focus back** (the RM-1312 / RM-1321 menu rules applied to a modal). `web/js/lib/escape_layers.js` `modalOpened(root, initial)` runs right after a modal is shown: it remembers the control that had focus (the opener; not when focus is already inside, so a re-open keeps the first opener) and focuses the close button. `modalClosing(root, refocus)` runs before it is hidden: with refocus, focus inside the modal or on the body goes back to the opener when it is still connected and rendered; otherwise focus inside the modal is dropped. Both `#task-modal` (`web/js/main.js` `openTaskModal` / `closeTaskModal`) and `#digest-popout` (`web/js/panels/right_now.js`) use them.
- Keyboard close = refocus: Escape (the dispatcher's `close`) and Enter / Space on the close button (a click with `detail === 0`). Pointer close = no refocus: a mouse click on the close button or on the scrim never parks focus on the opener, so the next Space on Replay is not taken by it (the RM-1321 reasoning).
- **Tab is held by the TOPMOST open layer only when it is `modal: true`.** A capture keydown listener in `escape_layers.js` cycles the modal's own rendered tabbables (`trapIndex`, wrapping both ways); from outside it (the body, after a click on the modal's text) the next Tab enters it. A layer painted over the modal (the digest popout at z 9500 over the task modal at z 200) holds Tab itself, and each Escape closes one layer and hands focus back one step.
- Both modals are now `role="dialog" aria-modal="true"` with `aria-labelledby` on their title (the task modal had no dialog role); `aria-modal` is only claimed because the focus is now actually held. `#task-modal-close` gained `aria-label="close"` (its name was the glyph). Both close buttons were under the hit floor (28x28 digest, 36x36 task) and are now `var(--hit-min)` square with a `--focus-ring` box-shadow on `:focus-visible`.
- Measured before the fix, headless Chromium 1920x920 under `?ui_mock=1`, opener `#view-trigger` focused, modal opened through its real path (a routed `/api/activity` row click; the digest icon click): focus stayed on the opener behind the scrim, Tab walked the page, Escape left focus on the body.
- Not changed: both openers are keyboard-unreachable (the digest icon is `role="button"` with an Enter / Space handler but no `tabindex`, and sits under the hit floor; an activity row is a bare clickable `span`). Filed as RM-1425 / RM-1426, not ruled here.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_modal_focus_open_close.py` (red 23 failed / 4 passed, green 27 passed), `trapIndex` and the no-document cases in `web/js/lib/escape_layers.test.mjs`.

## 2026-11-04 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 112 (RM-1421, RM-1423)

### RULING
- **Ctrl+K / Cmd+K belongs to the prompt only while the prompt is rendered and nothing modal or menu-shaped is over the page.** `web/js/main.js` `_promptRendered()` (`#input-text` has a client rect) gates the branch, together with `_menuRendered()` and `modalLayerRendered()`; otherwise the chord is not prevented and reaches the browser (whose own Ctrl+K is the address-bar search). A rendered prompt keeps the old behaviour, held keys included.
- Measured before the fix, headless Chromium 1920x920 under `?ui_mock=1`, focus on the body: on Home, Lobby, Session, History, Replay, Settings, Champ Select, Historical PGR, Build Insights and User Builds `#input-text` had no client rects (`#input-bar` is `display: none` on every view since 2026-04-25, `web/css/panels/input_activity.css`), Ctrl+K was `defaultPrevented` and focus stayed on the body. With the prompt given a box, Ctrl+K behind either open modal moved focus out of the modal to the input under the scrim (RM-1423).
- The keybinds toast (? / H) lists Ctrl+K only while the prompt is rendered (its row carries `data-key="ctrl-k"` and is `hidden` otherwise), so it no longer advertises a key that does nothing.
- Alternative not taken: make Ctrl+K reveal the hidden input bar. That reverses the 2026-04-25 operator ruling that hid it ("Ask Agent 7 wasn't working as intended"); not a lane call.
- Not measured: the Active Match / Last Match views (`#active-match` needs live data under `ui_mock`); the gate reads the input's box, not the view, so it holds there by construction.
- RM-1425 / RM-1426 are refuted as filed: neither opener can be reached by ANY input, mouse included. `#digest-icon` carries `hidden` in `web/index.html` and no script removes it (`renderDigest` only sets its class, label and title; `.digest-icon[hidden]` is `display: none !important`), and `#activity-strip` is `display: none` (`input_activity.css`, operator request 2026-04-29). So both modals hardened in cycles 110 / 111 have no production entry point. Whether to restore an entry point or retire the modals is filed for the operator (RM-1427), not ruled.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_ctrl_k_needs_a_rendered_prompt.py` (red 13 failed / 2 passed, green 15 passed).

## 2026-11-04 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 113 (RM-1422)

### RULING
- **A transient layer that a scroll carries out of sight closes.** A layer registered with `scrollAway()` in `web/js/lib/escape_layers.js` is watched by one capture `scroll` listener: when a scroll of an element (or the page) that holds it leaves it showing less area than the most it has shown since it opened (clip = the viewport plus every ancestor whose overflow is not visible), `scrollAway()` runs. A scroll that reveals more keeps it open, so a menu that opened partly cut off can still be scrolled into view. The opener calls `armScrollAway(name)` after showing it and after its focus move, so the focus scroll of the open is not a scroll away.
- Wired to the two layers that live in a scroller: the Lobby lane popup (`#lv-lane-popup`) and the Lobby mode menu (`#lv-mode-menu`), both position:absolute inside `#view-lobby` (`overflow-y: auto`). The view menu and the Home Find Match picker are position:fixed, so a scroll never moves them; they are not registered.
- Focus that was inside the closed layer goes back to its trigger with `preventScroll`, so the page stays where the wheel put it (a plain `focus()` would scroll the trigger back into view and undo the user's scroll).
- Measured before the fix, headless Chromium under `?ui_mock=1#lobby`, a real wheel (`page.mouse.wheel`, 600 px) over the view: `#view-lobby` scrolls 200 px at 1280x720 and 30 px at 923x1280 (0 at 1920x920). At 1280x720 the open lane popup went from y 110..208 to y -90..8 with the view's top edge at 57, still open, rendered, focus on its MIDDLE cell, so every letter shortcut stayed dead (`_menuRendered`). Same for the mode menu scrolled back up past what it had shown.
- Alternative not taken: keep the popup on-screen (reposition it on scroll). It is anchored to the lane pair; following a scroll would detach it from its trigger. Closing matches how a native select popup behaves.
- Alternative not taken: make `_menuRendered` count only an in-viewport menu. That leaves an open menu out of sight with the shortcuts live; it reopens on scroll-back already open, with focus lost in between.
- Not changed, filed: at 923x1280 the lane popup opens with its top 26 px above the view's top edge (popup top 30.8, view top 57), so its lane label row is cut off at open (RM-1428).
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_lobby_popup_closes_on_scroll.py` (red 6 failed / 2 passed, green 8 passed), `visibleArea` / `scrolledAway` / `armScrollAway` in `web/js/lib/escape_layers.test.mjs`.

## 2026-11-04 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 114 (RM-1428)

### RULING
- **The Lobby lane popup is whole inside the view at open.** `_openLanePopup` (`web/js/main.js`) places `#lv-lane-popup` above the lane pair as authored; when its top would sit above `#view-lobby`'s scroll origin (measured in CONTENT space: rect top minus the view's padding-box top plus `scrollTop`) it adds `.is-below` (`web/css/panels/header.css`: `top: calc(100% + 8px); bottom: auto`) and opens under the pair. The class is cleared on every open, so a resize between two opens never leaves a stale side. After the focus move the popup itself is scrolled into view (`block` / `inline` `nearest`), before `armScrollAway`, so the label row above the focused cell is never left past the view's edge.
- Measured before the fix, headless Chromium under `?ui_mock=1#lobby`: wherever the Lobby view stacks to one column (923x1280, 1024x600, 800x600) the pair's top is 79 px under the view's top and the popup needs 105 (97.2 tall plus the 8 px gap), so it opened at y 30.8 against a view top of 57 and its label row (y 41.8..61) was cut off where no scroll could reach. At 1920x920, 1280x720 and 1366x768 it fit (top 108.4..110.4) and keeps the above placement.
- Why content space, not the visible edge: a view merely scrolled down (1280x720 scrolled 200) has room above inside its content, so the popup still opens above and the open scrolls back to it, which the RM-1422 test `test_opening_from_a_scrolled_view_stays_open` pins.
- Alternative not taken: flip whenever the popup is cut by the VISIBLE edge. That flips a popup that only needs a scroll back, so the side would depend on scroll position instead of layout.
- Alternative not taken: move the lane pair down or pad the view top in the stacked layout. It changes the card layout for every user to fit a transient menu.
- Consequence for RM-1422: at 923x1280 the view scrolls only 30 px and the flipped popup stays whole after it, so it now correctly stays open; that test's second viewport moved to 1024x600 (the same stacked layout, 710 px of scroll), and its "the page stays where the wheel put it" check reads `min(600, maxScroll)` instead of assuming the wheel reached the end.
- 5-phase audit (headless, 1920x920 / 923x1280 / 1024x600): STRUCTURE popup inside the view, 0 px horizontal overflow on the document and the view, the popup paints on top at all 9 sampled points, never overlaps the lane pair; TYPOGRAPHY label 16px (`--fs-xs`), unchanged; HIT-TARGETS cells 46x46 against `--hit-min` 42px, keyboard-opened cell matches `:focus-visible` with a 2px solid ring; ASCII 0 non-ASCII bytes added; HIERARCHY label `oklch(0.94 0.02 305)` on the popup surface `oklch(0.27 0.06 302)`, unchanged.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_lobby_lane_popup_fits_at_open.py` (red 10 failed / 9 passed at HEAD before the scroll-away control was added, and 11 failed / 9 passed with it, measured by the verifier serving the HEAD main.js and header.css; green 20 passed).

## 2026-11-04 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 115 (RM-1429)

### RULING

After the fix at 1280x720 the open scrolls the view 28 px, the menu's bottom meets the view's bottom (677.4 against 677) and the trigger stays in sight (y 331.3..379.3).

Its sibling's comment is corrected the same way; behaviour unchanged.
- 5-phase audit (headless, 1280x720 / 1366x768 / 1920x920 / 1024x600): STRUCTURE menu and trigger inside the view, 0 px horizontal overflow on the document and the view, all 11 items paint on top at their centres; TYPOGRAPHY items 18px, section labels 16px (`--fs-xs` floor), unchanged; HIT-TARGETS items 43.6 px tall against `--hit-min` 42px, a keyboard-focused item matches `:focus-visible` with a 2px solid ring; ASCII 0 non-ASCII bytes added; HIERARCHY item text `oklch(0.72 0.045 305)` on the menu surface `oklch(0.27 0.06 302)`, unchanged.

## 2026-11-04 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 116 (RM-1430)

### RULING

The Home Find Match picker (`#home-find-match-picker`) is reachable whole at every window size and zoom: a `position: fixed` popover is capped at the window and scrolls its own content, and its queue groups fold into fewer columns rather than overflowing sideways.

- Measured before the fix, headless Chromium under `?ui_mock=1#home`: the picker is fixed, centred by `translate(-50%, -50%)`, `width: min(960px, 92vw)`, no max-height, overflow visible, on the 4-column `1fr` grid of `.lq-mode-menu`. A fixed box does not scroll with the page, so what falls outside the window is unreachable. 1920x920, 1366x768, 1280x720, 1024x600, 923x1280, 800x600 and 960x460 (200% zoom of the baseline) fit; the cycle-115 candidate (1024x600, 800x600) did not reproduce. At 768x368 (250% zoom) the picker ran from y -38.5 to 406.5 and Ranked Flex was cut off; at 640x307 (300%) 5 of 11 items stayed outside the window when focused; at 480x230 (400%) and 320x256 (the WCAG 1.4.10 reflow reference) 8 of 11, and the `1fr` columns stopped at their min-content width so the grid ran 47 px / 195 px past the picker's right edge.
- Fix (`web/css/panels/home.css`, `.home-find-match-picker.lq-mode-menu` only): `max-width` / `max-height: calc(100vw|100vh - 2 * var(--space-3))`, `overflow-y: auto`, `overscroll-behavior: contain`, `box-sizing: border-box`, and `grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(min(100%, 150px), 1fr))`. After: the picker sits at y 12 with a 12 px margin at 768x368 / 640x307 / 480x230 / 320x256, scrolls 101 / 328 / 405 / 621 px internally, and lays out 4 / 3 / 2 / 1 columns; a focused item is scrolled into view by the browser. At 1920x920, 923x1280 and 800x600 it is unchanged: 4 equal columns, 960x364 / 849.2x391, no internal scroll.
- Alternative not taken: a `@media (max-width / max-height)` block. auto-fit folds at the picker's own width, so the same rule holds for the 92vw and 960px caps without a breakpoint per viewport.
- Alternative not taken: shrink the item font or padding at small sizes. It would push text below the `--fs-md` the picker authors and the hit floor; the scroll keeps both.
- Not changed, the Lobby mode menu (`#lv-mode-menu`, `header.css`): it is absolute inside the scrolling `#view-lobby`, so its overflow is reachable by the view's scroll (RM-1429).
- Filed, pre-existing and separate: the Home page itself overflows the document sideways at narrow widths (22 / 182 / 342 px at 640 / 480 / 320 wide, measured with the picker closed). RM-1431.
- 5-phase audit (headless, 1920x920 / 923x1280 / 800x600 / 768x368 / 640x307 / 480x230 / 320x256): STRUCTURE the picker box and every focused item inside the window, 0 px horizontal overflow inside the picker; TYPOGRAPHY items 22px, section labels 18px, unchanged; HIT-TARGETS items 53 px tall (min) against `--hit-min` 42px at every size, the `:focus-visible` ring unchanged and not clipped (test_home_find_match_picker_focus.py); ASCII 0 non-ASCII bytes added; HIERARCHY item text `oklch(0.94 0.02 305)` on the picker surface `oklch(0.27 0.06 302)`, unchanged.

### RULING

Every row of the header view menu is reachable inside the window at every window size and zoom. Where the menu already fit, it keeps its authored height and never scrolls.

### RULING

When the header's current-view label is too narrow for "AUTO <dot> <VIEW>", the whole AUTO prefix gives way before any letter of the view name, and never part way. Where both fit nothing moves.

- Measured before the fix, headless Chromium under `?ui_mock=1&mode=sr`: since RM-1432 the label (`#view-current-label`, pinned 230 px, s218) may shrink below 230 px only under 720 px. At 320x256 it is 115 px (104 px of content). The label was one text node, and "AUTO <dot> " took about 56 px of it, so PRE-GAME LOBBY (185 px of text in a 115 px box), POST GAME REVIEW (202) and ACTIVE MATCH (168) showed 4 or 5 letters of the name. At 480x230 and wider the pair fits.
- Fix: `web/js/main.js` `_viewUpdateTitleLabel` writes the prefix and the name as two spans (`.title-current-auto`, `.title-current-name`) with createElement / textContent. The label's textContent, and so the trigger's accessible name, is unchanged. `web/css/panels/header.css`, inside the existing `@media (max-width: 720px)` block, makes the label a wrapping `row-reverse` flex row. The name is first in flex order (order -1), and the row is packed to the left, so it still reads AUTO then the name. When the pair does not fit, the whole prefix wraps to the clipped second line. The name alone ellipsizes inside the whole label.
- Why set the line height: the middle dot comes from a fallback font whose normal line is 17 px against the label font's 15 px. With `height: 1lh` the clip box was 15 px, shorter than the prefix line it had to show, and the probe read the prefix as hidden at 640. So the narrow label sets `line-height: 1.2; height: 1.2em`.
- After, at 320x256: the prefix is hidden whole, the name starts at the label's content edge, and PRE-GAME LOBBY shows 11 of its 13 non-space characters before its ellipsis (it showed 5). HOME, which fits, keeps "AUTO <dot> HOME". At 1920x920, 923x1280, 768, 640 and 480 the prefix and name both show whole on one line. The trigger stays 43 px tall everywhere.
- 5-phase audit (independent verifier on the headless render): STRUCTURE pass (the wrapped prefix is clipped and the label stays one line); TYPOGRAPHY pass (no new font-size, and the line-height rule applies only under 720 px); HIT-TARGETS pass (the trigger is 43 px and its focus-visible ring is unchanged); ASCII pass (header.css 9/9 non-ASCII bytes as at HEAD; main.js 215 -> 213, because tools/precommit_gate.py read the moved middle dot as net-new, so the prefix now writes it as `String.fromCharCode(0xB7)`, same rendered text); HIERARCHY pass (at 320 the view name is what shows). No MUST-FIX or SHOULD-FIX.
- FUTURE (NICE-TO-HAVE): a name still cut at 320 (POST GAME REVIEW) has no hover title. The open view menu marks the current row with aria-current, so the name can still be reached.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_view_label_prefix_gives_way.py`, 28 tests. It checks each character with Range rects, so the same probe reads the one-text-node label at HEAD. Red against the HEAD web files: 3 failed (320x256 lobby / last-match / active-match), 25 passed. Green: 28 passed. The live-half digest rows for header.css and main.js are re-captured (code changed, no glyph added).
- Filed, not fixed (RM-1439): `applyView` returns early when the target is the current view (main.js "if (viewId === _VIEW.current)"), so a manual pick of the view already shown, or "Auto (clear manual)" while the auto view equals the current one, never rewrites the label. The AUTO prefix then misstates the mode.

## 2026-11-05 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 122 (RM-1439)

### RULING

The header's current-view label states the view mode truthfully even when the view itself does not change: a manual view reads "<VIEW>", auto reads "AUTO <dot> <VIEW>", whichever path set the flag.

- Measured before the fix, headless Chromium under `?ui_mock=1&mode=sr`: `web/js/main.js` `applyView` returned early when the target was already the current view, and only the view-change path called `_viewUpdateTitleLabel`. From auto Home, a menu pick of Home saved `rc-view-manual=home` but the label kept "AUTO <dot> HOME". Opening on a saved manual Home (label "HOME") and picking "Auto (clear manual)" cleared the key but the label stayed "HOME", with no prefix.
- Fix: the same-view branch of `applyView` calls `_viewUpdateTitleLabel` too. `_viewUpdateTitleLabel` now returns without touching the DOM when the label's text already reads right (and the name span exists), because the same-view branch runs on every resolve, including each state envelope. A repeat pick of the shown view causes 0 label mutations.
- Default taken (recorded): a view reached by URL hash with no saved manual key still reads "AUTO <dot> <VIEW>", as before. The label follows the saved manual flag, not the hash. Not changed here. Note: `?ui_mock=1` with a `#view` hash clears `rc-view-manual` on load (the ui_mock IIFE), so a saved-manual fixture must load without a hash.
- 5-phase audit (independent verifier on the headless render, 1920x920): STRUCTURE (same two spans, no layout change); TYPOGRAPHY (no CSS change); HIT-TARGETS (trigger and menu rows unchanged); ASCII (main.js non-ASCII 213 bytes as at HEAD, 0 added); HIERARCHY (unchanged). See the cycle report for the verifier's verdict.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_view_label_same_view_pick.py`, 3 tests. Red against the HEAD web files: 3 failed. Green: 3 passed. The live-half digest row for main.js is re-captured (code changed, no glyph).

### RULING

A text that acts is a native `<button type="button">`, not a span with a click listener. ## 2026-11-05 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 126 (RM-1546)

### RULING

A focus ring is drawn in `--accent`, never in `--accent-2`. `--accent-2` is the theme's secondary pop: the red in ember and bloodmoon, a mid purple in arcane, so on those themes' dark red / purple surfaces it sits on or under the WCAG 1.4.11 3:1 floor. `--accent` is the Home / Lobby ring convention already (`.home-action-tile`, `.view-tab`, RM-1210; `.lq-btn`, `.lv-top8-tag`) and measures 5.17:1 or more on every adjacent surface in every theme. A ring that abuts its control (offset 0) is measured against the control's OWN fill as well as the surface outside it: the PAIR, both sides.

- Decision: reuse `--accent` (the two rings move to it) rather than mint a new per-theme ring token, as the RM-1546 filing suggested. Alternatives: (a) a new `--focus-ring-color` per theme - a second ring colour to keep in step with `--focus-ring` in four theme blocks, for two rules; (b) the existing `--focus-ring` box-shadow - an outline-offset ring cannot be expressed in it and the arcane active tab already draws its cyan underline as an inset box-shadow (themes.css), which a shadow ring would fight. Why: the convention exists, has headroom everywhere, and changes no token.
- Measured before the fix, headless Chromium under `?ui_mock=1#home` at 1920x920, computed colours resolved to sRGB: the focused ACTIVE mode tab's `--accent-2` ring against its own `--surface-3` fill was bloodmoon 2.56, ember 2.60, arcane 2.77 (below 3:1), and 3.39 / 3.46 / 3.75 against `#home-overlay` outside it; the focused Tonight's Pick champion name ring against the card `--surface-2` was bloodmoon 3.00, ember 3.05, arcane 3.28. hextech, moonlit and terminal were 7.8 to 10.6.
- After: both rings are the resolved `--accent` in all six themes, at least 5.17:1 against every adjacent COMPUTED background colour (lowest pair: arcane `--accent` on the active tab's `--surface-3` fill). Against the PAINTED pixels just outside the champion name ring (the independent auditor's pixel sample) the lowest is arcane 4.44:1, because the Tonight's Pick card carries a background gradient over its `--surface-2` colour; still above 3:1. The champion name TEXT stays `--accent-2`; the tab and card tints stay; only the rings moved. No other CSS rule draws an outline in `--accent-2` (scanned under `web/css`; the one other hit, `web/mock/addendum_a_concepts.html`, is a design mock the dashboard does not load).
- 5-phase audit (independent auditor on the headless render, 1920x920 and 923x1280, six themes): STRUCTURE (no ring clipped by an overflow ancestor; tab rings take 2 px of the 8 px gap and overlap no neighbour; no box moves on focus); TYPOGRAPHY (only the two outline colours changed); HIT-TARGETS (Tab reaches the four tabs and the name; tabs 44 px, name 42 px; `:focus-visible` matched, solid 2 px `--accent`); ASCII (0 non-ASCII / 0 CR in the touched files); HIERARCHY (ring vs the outside surface lowest arcane 6.99, vs the active tab fill lowest arcane 5.17; the ring is a different hue from the `--accent-2` name text in every theme, where before it was the same colour). PASS, no MUST-FIX. Its SHOULD-FIX 1 (the painted-gradient pair) is the qualification above.
- FUTURE (SHOULD-FIX): the guard measures the ring against the first opaque ancestor background COLOUR, so a background gradient (the Tonight's Pick card) is invisible to it; sampling the painted pixel beside the ring would close that gap for every ring guard of this shape.
- FUTURE (NICE-TO-HAVE): on the focused ACTIVE mode tab the `--accent` ring and the tab's own `--accent` inset underline are the same colour in five themes (arcane's underline is cyan), so the bottom edge reads as one thicker band; the ring still adds three new sides. A 2 px outline-offset would separate them but makes neighbouring tab rings touch in the 8 px gap.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_home_accent2_focus_ring.py`, 2 tests: every mode tab and the champion name, in every `:root[data-theme]` block plus the attribute-less hextech base, paint a solid 2 px `--accent` ring at 4.5:1 or more against the first opaque ancestor and, where the ring abuts the control, against its own fill (anchors: all themes measured, the flip changes `--accent`, the own-fill pair is read); and no `web/css` outline declaration names `--accent-2` (scanner anchored on at least 20 outline declarations). ### RULING

In a window 480 px wide or less, while voice is on, the footer connection label ("RC | <ws url>") steps out and the voice picker takes its room. With voice off, or above 480, the label stays.

- Decision (the RM-1438 design choice, adjudicated here): hide the LABEL, not the picker. Alternatives: (a) hide the picker under some width - removes the one in-footer way to change the voice while it is speaking (Settings still has one, but it is a view away); (b) a JS width check - a second mechanism for what one CSS rule says. Why 480: it is the 400% zoom tier of the baseline; at 481 to about 520 with voice on the label is still a 40 px "RC |" stub (FUTURE below), at 560 it is 119 px and at 640 199 px, so the label is kept wherever it shows real text.

- 5-phase audit (independent auditor on the headless render, #home and SR #active-match, voice off and on, 1920x920 / 923x1280 / 768x368 / 640x307 / 560x300 / 481x300 / 480x230 / 400x300 / 320x256, HEAD css served as the before): STRUCTURE (0 sideways scroll and the footer one 42 px row in all 72 states; label gone only at <= 480 with voice on); TYPOGRAPHY (every footer computed font size equal to before); HIT-TARGETS (toggle 42 px band, picker 42 px tall; Enter / Space toggle voice and focus stays on the toggle with `:focus-visible` while the label leaves); ASCII (0 non-ASCII / 0 CR added); HIERARCHY (health dot and CLIENT whole in every voice-on narrow state). PASS, no MUST-FIX. Under `?overlay=1` the footer is display:none, so the rule has no visible effect there.
- FUTURE (SHOULD-FIX, pre-existing): the picker's focus ring is `.voice-picker:focus` 1 px solid `--info` (header.css), while the footer buttons draw 2 px `--accent` on `:focus-visible`.
- FUTURE (NICE-TO-HAVE): at 481 to about 520 with voice on the label is still a 40 px "RC |" stub; a hard step against 480 where it is hidden. A label min-content floor would need a second rule for little gain.

The Active Match header zone pill reads at the `--fs-xs` floor (16 px), on one line, whole inside its pill. A text-only status pill whose label set is fixed sizes its slot for the widest label at the floor and never wraps; when a face renders wider than the measured one, that pill grows and does not wrap or clip.

Alternatives: (a) 21 px like the other pills (the font-uniformity rule) - "DEEP ENEMY" would need about 175 px, which costs the 1920 row and the narrow-window pill packing for a category call; (b) 16 px but keep 0.14em spacing - the label needs 135 px against a 120 px content box at desktop and wraps inside the 43 px pill; (c) widen the desktop slot - moves every pill to its right for every label. ## 2026-11-05 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 130 (RM-1549)

### RULING

Every Active Match header zone call (SAFE / DMZ / ENEMY ZONE / DEEP ENEMY) reaches WCAG AA 4.5:1 for its text on its own composited fill, in every theme. A status call painted as a semantic colour on its own translucent tint of that colour lifts the TEXT toward `--text` until the pair clears the bar; it never changes the semantic token or the tint, so every other `--bad` site is untouched.

- Decision: in `web/css/panels/header.css` the `.zone-pill.zone-enemy` text goes from `var(--bad)` to `color-mix(in oklab, var(--bad) 75%, var(--text))`; the `--bad-soft` fill, the zoneWarn pulse ring and the DEEP ENEMY solid `--bad` pill (map_state.css) are unchanged. Alternatives: (a) raise `--bad` per theme - moves dozens of `--bad` sites (history pips, grade badges, win pill, stale pills) for one call; (b) a stronger fill - a brighter red tint lowers the contrast against red text, the wrong direction; (c) a darker fill - the 18 percent tint is the shared `--*-soft` idiom of the SAFE / DMZ siblings, and darkening only this one breaks the set. Why: one declaration, the call keeps its red hue (red channel leads by 91 or more in every theme) and DEEP ENEMY keeps the stronger solid backing.
- Before (headless Chromium, `/?ui_mock=1&mode=sr&theme=<t>#active-match`, 1920x920, colours resolved through a canvas, the fill composited over the header's opaque `--canvas`; the pair, not the token): ENEMY ZONE text on fill arcane 4.11, hextech 4.13, moonlit 4.18, bloodmoon 4.43, ember 4.49 (under 4.5), terminal passed. (The cycle 129 row's 4.13 to 4.51 used a different composite rounding; same pairs.)
- After: ENEMY ZONE arcane 5.72, hextech 5.88, moonlit 5.90, bloodmoon 6.08, ember 6.14, terminal 6.73. Unchanged: SAFE 6.37 to 7.22, DMZ 5.86 to 7.88, DEEP ENEMY 4.88 to 5.64.
- 5-phase audit (headless render, 1920x920, six themes, four labels): STRUCTURE (colour only; slot map, one line and pill widths re-checked by test_zone_pill_text_floor.py, unchanged); TYPOGRAPHY (16 px weight 900 unchanged); HIT-TARGETS (not interactive); ASCII (0 non-ASCII and 0 CR added; header.css's 9 pre-existing non-ASCII bytes untouched); HIERARCHY (ENEMY ZONE still the red alarm hue, now a lighter red; DEEP ENEMY keeps the solid `--bad` backing with dark text, the stronger call; SAFE / DMZ untouched). No MUST-FIX. Closes the cycle 129 FUTURE (SHOULD-FIX) row on the ENEMY ZONE contrast.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_zone_pill_contrast.py`, 12 cases: six themes x every label at 4.5:1 or more on its composited fill, and six themes x the ENEMY ZONE text still red (red leads green and blue by 60 or more), not plain `--text`, its fill still `--bad-soft`, DEEP ENEMY still a solid fill. ## 2026-11-05 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 136 (RM-1554)

### RULING

`/ops.html` (the between-game Ops page: Seam Map, Drift Strip, Gated Queue, Match Ingest) renders on the dashboard's palette and theme, and every state-coloured text on it reaches AA 4.5:1 on its own fill.

- Found (measured, headless Chromium, `/ops.html` at 1920x920, `/api/ops/*` routed to synthetic payloads in the JS consumer's shapes): the page linked `tokens.css`, `themes.css` and `ops_panels.css` but not `panels/base.css`, where the default hextech palette lives at `:root` (themes.css header). So under hextech every palette token was undefined (lane doc trap 1): text computed black, the body and the `-soft` tints painted nothing, and no state colour showed at all. The page also never stamped `data-theme`, so it always fell to that unthemed base, whatever the dashboard theme (theme.js `DEFAULT_THEME` is arcane).
- Fix (`web/ops.html`): the index.html pre-paint theme guard is copied in (same whitelist, `rc-theme` key, `arcane` default; pinned against `web/js/lib/theme.js`), and `panels/base.css` is linked between `tokens.css` and `themes.css`. The inline body rule undoes base.css's dashboard-shell body (`display: block; height: auto; overflow: auto; user-select: text; letter-spacing: normal`), so the page still scrolls (1189 px of content in a 920 px window).
- Then measured with the palette resolved: every `--bad` text on the page was 3.01 to 4.48:1 on its own fill in all six themes. That is `.ops-fail` on `--bad-soft`, the inert seam row's name and its NO cell, the red drift pill's label and value, and the ingest fail word on `--surface` in arcane / moonlit. Fix (`web/css/panels/ops_panels.css`): those texts (plus `.ing-basis.ing-fail`, the same pair class) take the RM-1550 lift, `color-mix(in oklab, var(--bad) 70%, var(--text))`. Borders and tints stay `--bad` / `--bad-soft`. The drift pill label (`.strip .pill .k`) drops its `opacity: .85`, which alone left the red pill's label at 4.33:1 in arcane after the lift. Label and value stay apart by weight (400 vs 600).
- Decision: link base.css and undo its body rule, rather than copy the `:root` palette into the page or move it to tokens.css. Alternatives: (a) copy the palette - a second source of truth that drifts; (b) move the hextech `:root` block to tokens.css - touches the whole dashboard cascade for one page. Why: one palette source, and the page needs only five body declarations to stay a scrolling document.
- 5-phase audit (headless render at 1920x920, 1280x920 and 800x920, arcane and hextech): STRUCTURE (no sideways scroll at any width; the page scrolls vertically, body overflow auto); TYPOGRAPHY (0 visible text nodes under 16 px; h1 29 px, card titles 22 px, body text 16 px); HIT-TARGETS (the page has no interactive control; N/A); ASCII (ops.html and ops_panels.css 0 non-ASCII bytes, LF only); HIERARCHY (h1 brightest, accent card titles, state pills keep their hue: red reads red, amber amber, green green). See the cycle report for the verifier's verdict.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_ops_page_state_text_contrast.py`, 22 tests: every state text (fail card, the four drift pill states' label and value, inert seam name, NO and yes cells, the three ingest state words) at AA on its composited fill in all six themes, hue and tint kept, every palette token resolves in every theme, the unstamped load stamps theme.js `DEFAULT_THEME`, the page still scrolls, and the copied pre-paint guard matches theme.js and runs before the stylesheets, with base.css linked before themes.css. Red against HEAD's ops.html and ops_panels.css: 12 failed, 10 passed. The live-half digest rows for ops.html and ops_panels.css are re-captured (code changed, no glyph).

## 2026-11-05 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 137 (RM-1555)

### RULING

A click on a menu's non-focusable chrome (a divider, a section heading, the lane label, the padding between rows) keeps keyboard focus where it was, so the Escape that closes the menu hands focus back to its opener. A click inside a menu is never an outside click.

- Found (measured, headless Chromium, `?ui_mock=1`, 1920x920 and 923x1280), the same in all four `role="menu"` popups of `web/js/main.js`: the header view menu (`.view-menu-divider`), the Lobby mode menu (`.lq-mode-section`), the Home Find Match picker (the same headings) and the Lobby lane popup (`#lv-lane-popup-label`). The press moved focus to `<body>`. The focusout closers ignore a null related target, so the menu stayed open. The Escape that then closed it found no focus inside (each close helper refocuses only when `hadFocus`), so focus was left on `<body>`. The keyboard user lost their place in a menu they never left. On the view menu a pointer open did the same: the trigger held focus until the divider click took it. The Lobby mode menu was worse: its document click closer had no `contains` check, so a click on a heading closed the menu outright and dropped focus to `<body>`.
- Fix (`web/js/main.js`): one helper, `_holdFocusOnMenuChrome(menu)`, cancels the `mousedown` default when the press is not on a focusable control inside the menu. It is wired into the four menus. A press on a row, item or cell is left alone, so picks are unchanged. The Lobby document click closer now skips a click inside the mode menu, as the view menu's closer does and as the lane popup does by stopping its own clicks.
- Decision: cancel the mousedown, rather than make each menu container `tabindex="-1"`. Alternative (a), the container takes focus: the close helpers would then see `hadFocus`, but the arrow-key handlers look up the focused row and would go dead on the container, and Tab order changes. Alternative (b), refocus the opener on Escape even without `hadFocus`: that would steal focus after an outside-click route that left it elsewhere. Why: the menu pattern is "a press on the menu's chrome is not a focus move", and the fix needs no change to the four close helpers.
- Side effect, pinned: `<body>` focus with a menu still open was the setup of 26 existing tests (`test_hotkey_letter_owners.py`, `test_replay_keys_behind_menu.py`), reached by the divider / heading / label click. That route now keeps focus, so those tests reach `<body>` by a right-click on a bare spot outside the menu instead. A right-click fires no click (no outside-click close) and moves focus to nothing, the route `test_replay_keys_behind_menu.py` already pinned for its scroll test. Their assertions are unchanged, and the hotkey body-focus test now also covers the Lobby mode menu (it had no entry, because a click there used to close the menu).
- Also measured, no defect: the History / Session W / L counts and grade chips on a HOVERED row (the RM-1550 / -143 guards parked the pointer at 0,0). Lowest pair 4.64:1, grade D on a hovered neutral Session row in arcane. Pinned by `tests/snapshot_panels/test_history_row_hover_marks_contrast.py`, 25 tests.
- Closed, not painted: `.counter-line.negative` (`input_activity.css`) lives in the Adaptation panel inside the coaching `<main>`, which every `VIEW_IDS` entry hides (header.css). `.history-formstrip .pip.l` has no producer in `web/js`. No change. The other standalone pages: `vision_calibrator.html` declares its own palette, the `web/mock/*` pages and `legacy_index.html` are self-contained design mocks. Nothing else is missing base.css.
- 5-phase audit (headless, the four menus at 1920x920 and 923x1280): STRUCTURE (each menu still opens and closes as before; a chrome click leaves it open; no layout change); TYPOGRAPHY (no CSS change; N/A); HIT-TARGETS (keyboard reachability: Escape after a chrome click returns focus to the opener; row picks by pointer and keyboard unchanged, the existing focus-ring and keyboard guards green); ASCII (0 non-ASCII bytes added, LF only); HIERARCHY (no visual change; N/A).
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_menu_chrome_click_keeps_focus.py`, 12 tests: for each menu and both viewports, the chrome click lands on the named element (`elementFromPoint` anchor), leaves the menu open with focus unchanged, and Escape returns focus to the opener; plus two positive controls, a view menu row click still picks and a press on a lane cell's `<img>` still picks the lane (the guard's `closest()` finds the button). Red against HEAD's main.js: 10 failed, 2 passed (the two controls). The main.js live-half digest row is re-captured (code changed, no glyph).

## 2026-11-05 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 138 (RM-1556)

### RULING

An icon-only control is named by what it sets and what it holds, never by its icon alone. The Lobby lane triggers read "Primary lane: Middle", "Secondary lane: not set", "Secondary lane: not set, pick one" (needs-pick) and "Secondary lane: Fill, follows the primary lane" (Fill-locked).

- Found (measured, headless Chromium, `?ui_mock=1#lobby`, 1920x920 and 923x1280, names read through the accessibility tree): `#lv-lane-primary` / `#lv-lane-secondary` took their name from their content only, since `data-tt` is the app tooltip and not a name. A set lane was named by the icon alt, the uppercase `LANE_LABELS` value ("MIDDLE", "BOTTOM"), with no word of the slot. An unset slot was named "-". The needs-pick secondary had no text at all; its only content is the header.css `::before` glyph. The Fill-locked secondary showed the Fill icon with the img alt left at its HTML default "" (an EMPTY name) or at the last lane it held (a stale one). The two triggers side by side could not be told apart by a screen reader.
- Fix (`web/js/main.js`): `_renderLanePref` stamps `aria-label` from a new `_laneTriggerName(slot, pref, isFillLocked, needsPick)`, with title-case lane names (`LANE_NAMES`) that match the popup cells' alts. The Fill-locked icon alt is set to FILL. `web/index.html` carries "Primary lane: not set" / "Secondary lane: not set" as the pre-render default. The visible content (icon, dash, glyph) is unchanged.
- Decision: an `aria-label` on the button, rather than a visually hidden text span inside it. Alternative (a), a hidden span beside the icon: it needs a new utility class and the button's content changes per state anyway. Alternative (b), turn `data-tt` into `title`: the native tooltip would double the app tooltip. Why: one attribute, set where the state is decided, and the visible render is untouched.
- Not changed, filed FUTURE: the Fill-locked secondary still carries `aria-haspopup="menu"` but a press opens nothing (`main.js` returns early when the primary is Fill). Its name now says why; marking it `aria-disabled` is a separate call.
- 5-phase audit (headless, `#lobby` at 1920x920 and 923x1280): STRUCTURE (attribute-only change; no CSS keys on `aria-label`; layout unchanged, no page errors); TYPOGRAPHY (no CSS change; N/A); HIT-TARGETS (the triggers' size and focus ring unchanged, `test_lobby_queue_buttons_focus_ring.py` green; each now has a non-empty, slot-specific accessible name); ASCII (0 non-ASCII bytes added, LF only); HIERARCHY (no visual change; N/A).
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_lobby_lane_trigger_names.py`, 6 tests: the set-lane names at both viewports (with a control that "MIDDLE" / "BOTTOM" no longer name a button), the unset names (the visible dash kept), the Fill-locked name and its icon alt, the needs-pick name (built and read in one task, since the next lobby refresh resets it), and the index.html default. Lobby prefs are varied by routing `/data/ui_mock/lobby.json`. Red against HEAD's main.js and index.html: 6 failed. The main.js and index.html live-half digest rows are re-captured (code changed, no glyph).

## 2026-11-05 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 139 (RM-1759)

### RULING

A control state that changes what a press does, or that warns the operator, reaches the accessibility tree, not only the pixels. The Fill-locked Lobby secondary lane trigger is `aria-disabled="true"` and draws no hover affordance, and a party lane conflict is in the primary trigger's name: "Primary lane: Middle, conflicts with a party member".

- Found (measured, headless Chromium, `?ui_mock=1#lobby`, 1920x920 and 923x1280; the two FUTURE rows filed by cycle 138): (1) with the primary on Fill, `#lv-lane-secondary` shows the Fill icon and a press opens nothing (the `main.js` click handler returns early), but it kept `aria-haspopup="menu"` with no disabled state, so it was announced as a working menu button; its hover also drew the `--info` "clickable" border (`.lq-lane-pref:hover:not(:disabled)`, which out-ranks `.is-fill-locked`). (2) When the operator's primary equals a party member's primary, `_renderPartyMembers` adds `.is-conflict` to `#lv-lane-primary` (red border and a CSS `::after` "CONFLICT" overlay), while the name stayed "Primary lane: Middle". (3) `_renderPartyMembers` returned before its conflict pass on an empty member list, so a conflict from the last party stayed on the button.
- Fix (`web/js/main.js`): `_renderLanePref` sets `aria-disabled="true"` on the Fill-locked secondary and removes it in every other state. `_laneTriggerName` appends ", conflicts with a party member" to the primary while the new `_LV.laneConflict` is set. A new `_lvSetLaneConflict(on)` sets the flag, the class and the name together; `_renderPartyMembers` calls it after its conflict pass and with `false` on the empty-member path. It re-stamps the name because each lobby tick runs the lane render BEFORE the party render. `web/css/panels/header.css`: the lane hover rule adds `:not([aria-disabled="true"])`, and `.lq-lane-pref.is-fill-locked[aria-disabled="true"]:not(:disabled):not(.is-disabled) { opacity: 1; }`.
- Decision: `aria-disabled`, not native `disabled`. Alternative (a), native `disabled`: the button leaves the tab order and a keyboard user can no longer reach the name that says why it is locked. Alternative (b), drop `aria-haspopup` while locked: the button would still be announced as active with nothing behind it. Why: `aria-disabled` keeps focus and the name, and announces it unavailable.
- Decision: keep the s162 Fill-locked look (opacity 1). The RM-1139 `.lq-btn[aria-disabled="true"]` rule dims a held button to 0.55. Alternative: accept the dim. Why kept: the s162 design gave the locked state its own look (soft border, Fill icon at full strength, it reads as "Fill on both"), and this slice changes semantics, not looks. The hover affordance is removed (the `--info` border on a dead button was a false affordance). The override excludes `:disabled` / `.is-disabled`, so a no-LCU disabled trigger still dims to 0.55.
- Measured trap, for the next test that waits on a "refresh": under `ui_mock` no timer lobby tick runs while the SSE stub is fresh (`pollLcu`, 2000 ms, stands down) and the lobby fixture is fetched once, so a fixed wait spans no tick. A first draft of this guard waited 2600 ms and proved nothing (the verifier's MutationObserver saw 0 writes). The guard now forces a tick by switching the view to `#home` and back.
- FUTURE (NICE-TO-HAVE): hovering a CONFLICTED primary still swaps its red `--bad` border for `--info` (the hover rule out-ranks `.is-conflict`); the outline and the CONFLICT overlay stay red. Pre-existing, not changed here.
- 5-phase audit (headless, `#lobby` at 1920x920 and 923x1280, Fill-locked and conflict fixtures): STRUCTURE (no layout change, no horizontal overflow, no page errors); TYPOGRAPHY (no type change; N/A); HIT-TARGETS (both triggers 110x64, above `--hit-min`; both still take focus and match `:focus-visible` with the 2px solid ring, the Fill-locked one included; Enter on the Fill-locked secondary opens nothing); ASCII (0 non-ASCII bytes added, LF only); HIERARCHY (computed opacity 1 on both triggers, the Fill-locked border unchanged at rest and on hover, the conflict border `--bad` unchanged at rest).
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_lobby_lane_trigger_states.py`, 10 tests: the Fill-locked secondary is `aria-disabled` (read through the accessibility snapshot as `[disabled]`), not native disabled, opacity 1, and Enter keeps the popup closed, at both viewports; its hover leaves the border as at rest (control: the working primary's border changes); an empty party clears a stale conflict; a no-LCU disabled Fill-locked secondary still computes 0.55; neither trigger is `aria-disabled` when both work; leaving Fill clears it; the conflict name at both viewports, with every name value written across a forced second tick logged by a MutationObserver and required to carry the suffix; no conflict keeps the plain name. Lobby prefs, the mate's primary and the member list are varied by routing `/data/ui_mock/lobby.json`. Red against HEAD's main.js and header.css: 6 failed, 4 passed (the controls). Mutation: `_renderLanePref` ignoring `_LV.laneConflict` fails the conflict guard (an intermediate "Primary lane: Middle" is logged). The main.js and header.css live-half digest rows are re-captured (code changed, no glyph).

## 2026-11-05 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 140 (RM-1760)

### RULING

Hover never paints the --info "clickable" colour over a warning. A Lobby lane trigger in a warning state (party lane conflict on the primary, needs-pick on the secondary) keeps its --bad border while the pointer is on it. A toggle whose state is also in its own coloured text (Auto Accept On / Off, Party Open / Closed) keeps the --info hover.

- Found (measured, headless Chromium, `?ui_mock=1#lobby`, 1920x920 and 923x1280; the FUTURE row filed by RM-1759): `.lq-btn:hover:not(:disabled):not([aria-disabled="true"])` and `.lq-lane-pref:hover:...` are specificity (0,4,0) and out-rank `.lq-lane-pref.is-conflict` and `.lq-lane-pref.needs-pick` (0,2,0). Hovering the conflicted primary turned its red border `rgb(232, 64, 87)` to --info `rgb(124, 168, 255)` inside the still-red conflict outline; hovering the needs-pick secondary turned its dashed red border dashed blue, and that border is the state's main cue beside the red cross glyph.
- Closes the RM-1759 FUTURE (NICE-TO-HAVE) row "hovering a CONFLICTED primary still swaps its red `--bad` border for `--info`", and its needs-pick sibling.
- Fix (`web/css/panels/header.css`): `.lq-lane-pref.is-conflict:hover:not(:disabled):not([aria-disabled="true"])` and the same for `.needs-pick` restate `border-color: var(--bad)` at (0,5,0). No JS change.
- Decision: the warning states get no other hover cue (cursor stays `pointer`, the press still opens the lane popup). Alternative (a), a hover background tint: the queue strip has no surface token lighter than `--surface-alt`, the buttons' resting fill, so it needs a new token. Alternative (b), an --info box-shadow ring: it would sit on top of the 1px conflict outline and repeat the mixed signal. Why: the warning colour is the information; the hover cue is secondary.
- Decision: the Auto Accept and Party toggles are not changed. Their state is in line2 text and its colour, which hover leaves alone (pinned by a test); the --info border on them is a true affordance.
- 5-phase audit (headless, `#lobby`, conflict fixture, at rest, hovered and hovered + keyboard-focused, before and after the CSS change): STRUCTURE (the four queue-strip controls' rects identical, no horizontal overflow, no page errors); TYPOGRAPHY (font sizes identical; N/A); HIT-TARGETS (triggers 110x64, `cursor: pointer`; hovered + focused primary matches `:focus-visible` with the 2px --accent ring); ASCII (0 non-ASCII bytes and 0 CR added); HIERARCHY (the only difference between the before and after captures is the hovered primary's border colour, --info to --bad; accessibility tree identical, the conflict name intact).
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_lobby_lane_warning_hover.py`, 5 tests: the conflicted primary's border and outline stay --bad on hover at both viewports, and hovered + keyboard-focused it keeps the --bad border under the 2px --accent ring; the needs-pick secondary's border stays --bad and dashed on hover; control, a plain trigger still turns --info on hover; control, Auto Accept and Party still turn --info and their line2 text and colour are unchanged on hover. Border colours are read with the `.lq-btn` transition off. Red against HEAD's header.css: 3 failed, 2 passed (the controls). The header.css live-half digest row is re-captured (code changed, no glyph).

## 2026-11-05 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 141 (RM-1761)

### RULING

A keyboard sort keeps the operator's place. A Build Insights sort header that re-sorts its table keeps keyboard focus (and its focus ring) on the same column header, so a second press flips the order and Tab continues to the next header. A held Enter or Space is one sort.

- Found (measured, headless Chromium, `?ui_mock=1#build-insights`, 1920x920 and 923x1280): `web/js/panels/build_insights.js` `_render` rebuilds the whole table with `innerHTML` on every sort. Enter or Space on a focused `th.bi-sortable` sorted the table and threw focus to `<body>` (lane doc trap 2). A second press did nothing, and the next Tab started from the top of the page, so a keyboard user could sort once per Tab walk. With focus kept, a held key would re-sort on every autorepeat and flip the order back and forth (RM-1758 (a)).
- Fix (`web/js/panels/build_insights.js`): `_render` captures the focused header's `data-sort` key before the rebuild and re-focuses that header after it (`_captureSortFocus` / `_restoreSortFocus`, the `overlay_layout.js` capture / restore pattern; null when focus was not inside the table, so a re-render never steals focus). The header keydown still calls `preventDefault` on Enter / Space but skips the sort on `e.repeat` (RM-1322).
- Decision: keep focus by key in `_render`, not only in the key handler. Alternative (a), re-focus in the keydown handler after `handler()`: it misses the click path, where the clicked header also holds focus (tabindex 0) and was destroyed the same way. Alternative (b), sort the rows in place and leave the header nodes alone: a larger rewrite of the table builder for the same result. Why: one place owns the rebuild, so one place carries the focus.
- Closes RM-1758 (a) (the held-key re-sort; it was read, not run, in cycle 103; with focus lost it could not fire, with focus kept it would have).
- FUTURE (SHOULD-FIX, pre-existing): the sort state is not exposed to assistive tech. The headers are `<th role="button">`, so `aria-sort` does not apply, and the state is the visible letter " v" / " ^" inside the name ("WPA v"). The APG sortable-table shape is a `<button>` inside a plain `<th aria-sort>`; it touches the `th.bi-sortable` selectors in build_insights.css and two guards, so it is a slice of its own.
- FUTURE (NICE-TO-HAVE, pre-existing): the loading and empty branches of `_render` replace the table without the focus carry, so a data refresh that lands while a header holds focus still drops it to `<body>`. Refreshes start from the tab buttons or the min-games input (focus outside the table), so it is not reached today.
- 5-phase audit (headless render, 1920x920 and 923x1280): STRUCTURE (no layout change, no horizontal overflow, no page errors); TYPOGRAPHY (no CSS change; headers 16 px); HIT-TARGETS (headers 42 px tall; after a keyboard sort the same header holds focus and matches `:focus-visible`, and Tab moves to the next header, WPA to the count column); ASCII (0 non-ASCII bytes and 0 CR in the touched files); HIERARCHY (no visual change; N/A).
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_build_insights_sort_header_focus.py`, 8 tests: Enter and Space at both viewports keep focus on the sorted header with the ring, a second press flips the order, and Tab then reaches the next header; a held Enter (three `repeat: true` keydowns) is one sort at both viewports; controls, a mouse click still sorts and flips, and a re-render with focus in the min-games input leaves focus there. Red against HEAD's build_insights.js: 6 failed, 2 passed (the controls). Mutation: the sort run on every keydown (no `e.repeat` check) fails the two held-key tests. The live-half digest row for build_insights.js (tests/guard_data/web_live_half/js_panels.txt) is re-captured (code changed, no glyph).

## 2026-11-05 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 142 (RM-1762)

### RULING

A sortable table says which column is sorted, and which way, in the accessibility tree. A Build Insights sort header is the APG sortable-table shape: a plain `<th>` (role columnheader) that holds a `<button type="button" class="bi-sort-btn">`, with `aria-sort="ascending"` / `"descending"` on the sorted column's cell only, and the visible arrow letter `aria-hidden`, so the button is named by the column alone.

- Found (measured, headless Chromium, `?ui_mock=1#build-insights`, 1920x920 and 923x1280; the RM-1761 FUTURE row): each sortable header was `<th tabindex="0" role="button">`. The role override took the cell out of the column-header role, so `aria-sort` could not apply, and no header carried it. The only sort cue was the letter " v" / " ^" appended to the text, so the button was announced "WPA v": a stray letter, no direction, and no "sorted" state.
- Fix (`web/js/panels/build_insights.js`): a new `_sortHeadHtml` builds the three sortable cells; `_wireHeaders` binds click and keydown to the inner button; `_restoreSortFocus` re-focuses the button (the RM-1761 carry, still keyed by the cell's `data-sort`). The keydown `preventDefault` on Enter / Space is now also what stops the native button's own activation, so a press is one sort (a mutation without it fails 6 tests: the first Enter sorted twice). `web/css/panels/build_insights.css`: the cell gives its padding to the button (`padding: 0`, height `--hit-min`); `.bi-sort-btn` is a block, full width, `min-height: var(--hit-min)`, inherits font, colour, case, letter-spacing and alignment, and carries the hover colour and the inset `--focus-ring` that the cell carried.
- Decision: a button inside the cell, not `aria-sort` on a focusable cell without a role. Alternative (a), drop `role="button"` and keep the cell as the tab stop: `aria-sort` becomes valid, but a focusable column header is not announced as something you can press. Alternative (b), keep `role="button"` and add a visually hidden "sorted descending" text: the state lives in a name, not in the column, so it is not read as the table's sort. Why: the APG pattern is the one shape screen readers announce as both a pressable control and a sorted column.
- Decision: the header row is 43 px tall, was 42. With `border-collapse: collapse` the 2px header border puts 1 px inside the cell, and the button itself now meets `--hit-min` (42 px). Alternative: `min-height: calc(var(--hit-min) - 1px)` to keep the old row height, which puts the actual press target below the floor. Why: the floor is the control's, not the cell's; one px of header height is not a layout change (column x / width identical, no overflow).
- Closes the RM-1761 FUTURE (SHOULD-FIX) row "the sort state is not exposed to assistive tech".
- 5-phase audit (headless render, 1920x920 and 923x1280, before and after the change, same probe): STRUCTURE (every header's x and width identical, row 42 -> 43 px, table width identical, no horizontal overflow, no page errors); TYPOGRAPHY (computed font size 16 px, weight, family, colour, uppercase, letter-spacing, alignment and padding identical on all five headers); HIT-TARGETS (the three buttons are 42 px tall and fill their cell's width; keyboard focus lands on the button and matches `:focus-visible` with the inset 2px ring, `cursor: pointer` on the three sortable headers and `auto` on the two plain ones, as before); ASCII (0 non-ASCII bytes and 0 CR in the touched files); HIERARCHY (no visual change).
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_build_insights_sort_header_aria.py`, 6 tests: at both viewports the three sortable cells have no role override and no tabindex and hold a `type="button"` button that fills the cell and is at least 42 px tall, and the accessibility tree has column headers "WPA" and "Win Rate" and buttons named exactly "WPA" / "Win Rate" (three buttons in the table); at both viewports `aria-sort` starts as `{wpa: descending}` only, follows an Enter to `{n: descending}` and a Space to `{n: ascending}`; on the Runes tab a click sets `{observed_winrate: descending}`; control, the "Expected WP" header stays a plain column header with no button and no `aria-sort`. Red against HEAD's build_insights.js and build_insights.css: 5 failed, 1 passed (the control). The RM-1761 guard (`test_build_insights_sort_header_focus.py`) and `test_focus_ring_token_sites.py` now read focus and the ring off `.bi-sort-btn`. The live-half digest rows for build_insights.js (js_panels.txt) and build_insights.css (css_panels.txt) are re-captured (code changed, no glyph).
- FUTURE (NICE-TO-HAVE, from the verifier): the bottom 1 px of each sortable cell (the collapsed border line, under the 42 px button) no longer sorts on click and shows `cursor: auto`; before, the whole cell was the target. A cell click listener forwarding to the button would close it.
- FUTURE (SHOULD-FIX, pre-existing, from the verifier): the sort header hover is barely visible in the default theme. Measured at rest `oklch(0.74 0.045 305)` (`--dim`) and hovered `oklch(0.72 0.045 305)` (`--accent`): same hue and chroma, 0.02 lightness apart. Same before this change; a theme-token question, not this slice.

## 2026-11-05 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 143 (RM-1763)

### RULING

A hover affordance is a visible step in EVERY theme, measured on resolved colours, not a token swap that happens to read in the gold themes. A Build Insights sort header hovers from `--dim` to `--text` (the brightest ink), the same as the sibling `.bi-tab:hover` in the same file; the step must be brighter than rest and at least 1.5:1 in luminance ratio in all six themes.

- Found (measured, headless Chromium, `?ui_mock=1&theme=<t>#build-insights`, 1920x920; colours resolved to sRGB through a 1x1 canvas because several tokens are oklch(); the RM-1762 FUTURE row): `.bi-sort-btn:hover` set `color: var(--accent)`. Rest -> hover luminance ratio per theme: hextech 1.16, terminal 1.00 (DARKER, hue only), ember 1.24, bloodmoon 1.25, moonlit 1.30, arcane 1.07 (DARKER, same hue). In arcane, the operator default, `--accent` is the lavender-silver frame metal, so the header went from rgb(177,164,194) to rgb(171,158,188): no hover cue at all.
- Rejected alternatives: a per-theme override for arcane only (it fixes one theme and leaves terminal's 1.00 hue-only step, and a new theme would repeat the defect); a hover background tint (`--surface-2`, the body row hover) on top of the colour (it would make a hovered header read like a selected row; not needed once the ink step is >= 1.95).
- Fix (`web/css/panels/build_insights.css`): `.bi-table .bi-sort-btn:hover { color: var(--text); }`. Ratios after: hextech 2.29, terminal 2.05, ember 2.01, bloodmoon 2.01, moonlit 1.97, arcane 1.95. The hextech hover loses its gold hue; accepted, since gold stays the active-tab fill and the sort letter is the state cue.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_build_insights_sort_header_hover.py`, 9 tests: per theme (6) the resting colour equals `--dim`, the hovered colour is brighter, at least 1.5:1 over rest and equals `--text`; in arcane at both viewports the step holds and moving off restores rest; control, the non-sortable "Expected WP" header has no button and keeps `--dim` on hover. Red against HEAD's build_insights.css: 8 failed, 1 passed (the control). The css_panels.txt live-half digest row is re-captured (code changed, no glyph).
- Closes the RM-1762 FUTURE (SHOULD-FIX) row "the sort header hover is barely visible in the default theme".

In arcane, the operator default, `--accent` rgb(171,158,188) on the lavender tint rgb(72,61,89) is under AA 4.5 for the 18px bold label (WCAG large text starts at 18.66px bold).

Moonlit sits at the 1.5 floor (a small moonlit `--text` / `--accent` move turns the guard red, which is the intent).

## 2026-11-05 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 145 (RM-1765)

### RULING

A global letter shortcut (A, F, Z, H, ?, Shift+R) yields to a focused control by what the control READS, not by its tag: an `<input>` keeps the letters only when its type takes text. A checkbox, radio, range, color or button-type input reads no letter key, so the shortcut acts from it, as it does from a `<button>`. `web/js/main.js` `_letterShortcutsYield` now routes through `review_player_model.js` `isTypingTarget` (already imported for the RM-1366 Space rule), so the global hotkeys and the Replay player draw the line in the same place. A `<select>`, a `<textarea>`, a contenteditable host and every text-entry input type (no type, text, search, email, password, url, tel; also number and date, untested here) still take the letters.

- Found (measured, headless Chromium, `?ui_mock=1#home`, `#settings`, `#replay`, 1920x920; picked up from RM-1758 (c)): with focus on a checkbox, radio, range, button, submit, reset or color input, none of the six shortcuts fired (42 of 42 synthetic cases red), and the same on the real Settings "Auto-push runes" toggle (`#set-push-runes`), the Settings PGR baseline slider (`#set-pgr-baseline`) and the Replay items filter (`#replay-evt-items`) for A, Z and H (9 of 9 red). Ticking a Settings toggle from the keyboard left every shortcut dead until focus moved. The RM-1323 tag test (INPUT / TEXTAREA / SELECT) was wider than its own stated rule.
- RM-1758 (c), held keys in a text control: A, F, Z, H, ? and Shift+R held (one press plus 3 autorepeats) in an input, a textarea and a contenteditable host each type 4 characters and fire no shortcut. Already true at HEAD (18 of 18 green before the fix); now pinned. Closes RM-1758 (c).
- Rejected alternatives: add a `type` check inline in `_letterShortcutsYield` (a second copy of the non-text type list that drifts from the Replay player's); keep the tag test and document it as intended (contradicts the RM-1323 rule text and the "ordinary button" positive control).
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_letter_shortcuts_input_kinds.py`, 105 tests: every shortcut acts from each of 7 non-text input types (42); A, Z and H act from the 3 real hosts and leave their checked / value unchanged (9); every letter is text in each of 6 text input types (36); every letter held in an input, a textarea and a contenteditable types every repeat and fires nothing (18). Red against HEAD's main.js: 51 failed, 54 passed. The js_main.txt live-half digest row is re-captured (code and comment changed, no glyph).
- 5-phase audit: keyboard behaviour only, no rendered change. STRUCTURE / TYPOGRAPHY / HIERARCHY: no CSS or DOM change. HIT-TARGETS (keyboard reachability): the shortcuts are reachable again from the Settings and Replay toggles and slider. ASCII: 0 non-ASCII bytes added, 0 CR in the touched files.

## 2026-11-05 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 146 (RM-1766)

### RULING

A grid of named chips drops a column before it cuts a name. The TFT Active Match BOARD (`#am-build-body .am-tft-board`) keeps at most five columns, and a unit chip is never narrower than 6 x `--fs-sm` (108 px) unless the board itself is narrower; the 1920x920 baseline keeps its five 160 px columns.

- Found (measured, headless Chromium, `?ui_mock=1&mode=tft_round#active-match`, the recorded seven-unit S134 round row; a scratch audit of the TFT views at 1920x920 and 923x1280 flagged it): `web/css/panels/active_match.css` set `grid-template-columns: repeat(5, minmax(0, 1fr))`, so the chips shrank with the BOARD pane and the names ellipsised. Chip width and cut names by viewport: 1440 115 px ("Fiddlesticks" 82/109); 1199 93 px ("Yorick" 49/55); 1024 77 px (all six names over two letters cut, "Rakan" 33/45); 923x1280 (the companion size) 67 px ("Rakan" and "Yorick" 24 px, i.e. two letters); 768 53 px ("Rakan" 9 px); 640 41 px (every name 0 to 7 px); 480 26 px and 320 11 px (every name 0 px, and at 320 a chip spilled past the board).
- Fix (CSS only, `web/css/panels/active_match.css`): `repeat(auto-fill, minmax(max(min(calc(var(--fs-sm) * 6), 100%), calc((100% - 4 * 6px) / 5)), 1fr))`. The `/ 5` term caps the board at five columns (wide panes are byte-identical to before); the `--fs-sm * 6` term is the chip floor, sized for a six-letter name plus a two-star mark (Yorick 55 px + 6 px gap + "**" 19 px inside 18 px of padding and border); `min(..., 100%)` keeps a one-column board inside a pane narrower than the floor.
- After: 1920 5 x 160 px (unchanged); 1440 5 x 115 px; 1199 4 x 118 px; 1024 3 x 132 px; 923 3 x 116 px; 768 2 x 141 px; 640 2 x 111 px; 480 1 x 153 px; 320 1 column the board's width. Every name of six letters or fewer is whole wherever the board is at least one chip wide; "Fiddlesticks" (109 px) still ellipsises below about 130 px chips, and the chip's `title` / `aria-label` carry it whole, as before.
- Decision: a narrower window shows fewer units at once instead of every unit illegibly. The wrapper keeps its fixed two-chip-row height (132 px; no reflow when a board lands, the S134 rule), so at 1199 and under a seven-unit board scrolls inside it (it was already the named `region` "Your board" with a focus ring for an over-full board). Alternatives: (a) let the wrapper grow with the row count at narrow widths - breaks the same-box rule for the empty and filled states and pushes the pane row; (b) a smaller name font at narrow widths - goes under `--fs-sm` for the brightest text in the pane; (c) keep five and wrap the name onto two lines - doubles the chip height and still cuts at 640. Why: a cut name is no name; scrolling inside a named region is the documented over-full behaviour.
- 5-phase audit (independent verifier, headless, `tft_round` at 1920x920 and 923x1280, HEAD's rule injected as the before): STRUCTURE (no page overflow, every chip inside the board; wrapper 132 px and the BUILD pane 202.4 / 203.0 px in both before and after; at 923 the third chip row, 189 px of content in the 132 px box, scrolls inside); TYPOGRAPHY (names 18 px, items 16 px, unchanged); HIT-TARGETS (display-only list; at 923 the scrolling wrapper is reached by Tab and matches `:focus-visible` with the 2 px `--accent` ring; at 1920 it does not scroll and is not a tab stop); ASCII (0 non-ASCII bytes and 0 CR in the 4 touched files); HIERARCHY (unit names `--text`, relative luminance 0.825, the brightest text in the pane; stars 0.437, items and eyebrows 0.398).
- FUTURE (SHOULD-FIX, from the verifier): at 1199 and under a seven-unit board hides its last row with no visible sign that more units sit below (a bottom fade or a "+N" cue would say so).
- FUTURE (NICE-TO-HAVE, from the verifier): the wrapper is a tab stop only because Chromium makes a scrolling box keyboard-focusable by itself (its `tabIndex` reads -1); an explicit `tabindex="0"` while it overflows would not depend on the rc-shell Chromium version.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_tft_board_unit_columns.py`, 22 cases: the baseline control (five 160 px columns, two rows inside the wrapper, every name whole); nine sizes 1920 to 320 x (at most five columns; every chip at least min(108 px, board width); every chip inside the board; no page overflow); the same nine x (every name of six letters or fewer whole; skipped where the board is narrower than one chip, 320 px; every chip's `title` and `aria-label` carry the name); three sizes x the wrapper still 132 px and still the named region. Red against HEAD's active_match.css: 13 failed, 8 passed, 1 skipped. The css_panels.txt live-half digest row is re-captured (code and comment changed, no glyph).

### RULING

A row's redundant picture gives way before its value.
