## 2026-11-05 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 149 (RM-1769)

### RULING

An overlay canvas is shown only while it lies exactly over the image it annotates, and it is re-fitted by a ResizeObserver, not by its data poll. The Active Match MAP pane's `#am-map-overlay` (web/js/panels/active_match.js `_renderAmMap`) now starts `display: none`; `_amFitOverlay` lays it over the rendered `#am-map-img` box and shows it, or hides it when there is no image (loading, or the `am-map-noimg` branch); a ResizeObserver on the figure and the image re-fits and re-draws (`_amDrawDots` with the cached vision state) on any size change. `_amDrawOverlay` is now the text layers plus `_amDrawDots`.

- Before (measured headless on the sr / aram ui_mock fixtures): the canvas was created with no size, so it was a default 300 x 150 box at the figure's top-left until a `/api/vision-state` answer ran `_amDrawOverlay`. With no base image it stayed for good and ran past a narrow figure (the 148 cycle's FUTURE row: 21 / 106 px past the MAP pane at 640 / 480), and the pane body (overflow-y: auto, so overflow-x auto too) gained a horizontal scroller: `#am-map-body` scrollWidth - clientWidth 22 / 107 px at 640 / 480 at HEAD, 0 after. With an image but no vision answer, it sat off the map. Even with vision, a figure re-layout after the last draw left it off the image until the next 500 ms poll (measured: 53 px above the image at 1920x920).
- This corrects the 148 FUTURE row's diagnosis: with an image and a vision answer, the canvas did track the image box (512 / 469 / 333 / 264 / 179 px at 1920 / 1024 / 768 / 640 / 480), so dots were offset only between polls. The fixed 300 px box was the unsized default, not a fixed width.
- Alternatives: (a) CSS `max-width: 100%` on the canvas - it caps the box but still shows a 300 x 150 rect off the image, and the pixel buffer still needs JS; (b) wrap the image and canvas in an inline-block stage and `inset: 0` the canvas - a percentage `max-height` on the image would not resolve inside an auto-height wrapper, so the image would no longer cap to the figure (reasoned from the CSS percentage rule, not measured); (c) re-fit on `window.resize` - it misses the pane re-laying out as other panes fill, which was the 53 px case.
- Sibling, not changed: the legacy minimap's `#mm-vt-overlay` (web/index.html, sized in web/js/main.js) uses the same JS-sized pattern inside a wrapper that stays hidden until shown. FUTURE: probe it for the same default-box window.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_am_map_overlay_canvas_fits.py`, 47 cases. With a generated 512 px map image, at five sizes 1920 to 480, sr and aram, vision answering or failing (503): the canvas covers the image box to within 1 px and stays inside the figure, and the pane body has no x-overflow. A resize from 1920 and from 1024 down to 640 and 480 and back re-fits within 300 ms, under the poll period, with or without vision. ## 2026-11-05 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 150 (RM-1770)

### RULING

The dashboard Active Match view fits the window below the header: CALL, BUILD and MAP all start and end inside the view, and the view never scrolls. The BUILD pane scrolls as a whole; CALL and MAP scroll inside their pane bodies, as authored. `body[data-view="active-match"] #view-active-match` is a flex column outside the overlay shell (web/css/panels/active_match.css; the RM-1063 Replay pattern), so `.am-grid`'s own `flex: 1 1 auto; min-height: 0` takes the height under the view head and the two `1fr` rows are definite. The BUILD pane (`.am-pane-build`) scrolls as a whole, with `overscroll-behavior: contain`. Its `.am-pane-head` is sticky at the top (z-index 2, opaque `--surface`), and `#am-build-body` keeps its natural height (`flex: 1 0 auto`, overflow visible).

- Before (measured headless, ui_mock, no map image, vision 503):

  - That size came from the MAP pane, which spans both rows and whose shell (`height: 100%`) and figure (`max-height: 50%`) are percent heights with no definite base.
  - At 1920x920 (sr): grid 3139 px tall in an 823 px view, rows `1562.55px 1562.55px`, BUILD pane top at y = 1680 (760 px below the window), and a 1563 px CALL pane around about 380 px of content. The view scrolled 3189 into 821.
  - The same in aram (2981 px) and arena (2949 px), and at 1280x720 (3219 px) and 923x1280 (3876 px). TFT has no map figure and already fit.
  - The R213 entry (LEDGER 1090) measured this inflation in 2026-07 ("the section already scrolls 3610px into 1003px") and left it as context. This ruling closes it.
- Why not a flex column alone: the BUILD pane holds the DS card cluster (spike curve, matchup, capability gap, combo, relscore, knobs, statcheck) ABOVE `#am-build-body`, in a pane that is `overflow: hidden`. With a definite row, the body (`flex: 1 1 auto; min-height: 0`) was squeezed to 28 px of its 453 (measured, sr 1920x920), and the ranks scrolled in a sliver.
- After, at 1920x920: rows `378.594px 378.609px`, grid 771 px inside the 823 px view, and the view scroll is 0. BUILD content (about 1500 px in the sr fixture) scrolls inside its 379 px pane under the pinned head (elementFromPoint at the head after a 120 px scroll returns the head). The MAP figure caps at 50% of its pane as authored (358 px). TFT rows grow from 202 to 378 px, filling the window.

  - (a) Keep the page scroll and only stop the MAP inflation (for example `height: auto` on the shell). The rows stay `1fr 1fr` of a content-sized grid, so both rows equal the taller of CALL and BUILD (BUILD about 1500 px in sr). BUILD would still start below the fold behind an empty CALL pane. Reasoned from the grid row rule, not measured.

## 2026-11-05 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 151 (RM-1771)

### RULING

The TFT Active Match BOARD says how many units sit below its fixed box. The board eyebrow row (`.am-tft-head`, web/js/panels/active_match.js `_amTftBoardNode`) now holds the count line (`.am-tft-sec`, unchanged text) and a cue, `.am-tft-more`, reading "+N BELOW", where N is the number of unit chips not wholly inside the visible wrapper. The cue shows only when N > 0. It follows the box: a `scroll` listener on the wrapper and one ResizeObserver on the wrapper and the list (a dropped column is a taller list in the same box), so a window resize shows or hides it with no new round and no poll. One observer at a time: a rebuilt board replaces it, and leaving the TFT fallback releases it (`_amTftStopMore`).

- Before (measured headless, ui_mock tft_round, seven units): since RM-1766 a narrower window drops a column instead of cutting a name, so the fixed eyebrow + two-row box (132 px) holds 10 units at five columns, 8 at four, but 6 at three. Hidden below the box with nothing on screen saying so: 1 unit at 1100 / 1024 / the 923x1280 companion size (three columns), 3 at 800-640 (two), 5 at 560 and under (one). The eyebrow read "7 UNITS - as of 17:27" throughout. 1920 to 1199 fit (five or four columns).
- Placement: in the eyebrow's reserved line, right of the count, so the box never grows (no reflow, the U-156 fixed-height rule) and the cue never covers a chip. The count text ellipsises first (its title carries it whole, RM-1767); the cue stays whole from a 150 px board up. In a board narrower than the cue itself (a 320 px window, 78 px board) it ellipsises inside the box instead of spilling into a sideways scroll (`max-width: calc(100% - 12px)`).
- Pinned: the eyebrow row is `position: sticky; top: 0` with the pane colour (`--surface-head`) behind it and its 6 px gap as padding, so the cue stays on screen while units remain below and a chip scrolling under it never shows through. Without it (the verifier's SHOULD-FIX on the first pass) scrolling one row at 480 px scrolled the cue away with 4 units still below.
- Wording: "+N BELOW", short on purpose. The eyebrow already says UNITS, and "+5 UNITS BELOW" (about 170 px) would not fit the 153 px board at 480 px. The cue's title spells it out ("5 more units below - scroll the board").
- Type and colour: the eyebrow's own (`--fs-xs` 16 px, 700, `--text-dim`), a cue, not a headline. Measured at 923x1280 on the board's resolved opaque background: cue 6.57:1 (arcane, the default), 6.90 (bloodmoon), 6.71 (ember), 6.81 (moonlit), 8.18 (terminal); unit names 12.83-16.78:1 on their chip, so the names stay the brightest text.
- aria-hidden: the list already gives assistive tech every unit; the cue is a visual scroll affordance. It is a span, not focusable, nothing to click.
- Alternatives:
  - (a) A sticky "+N" pill at the bottom of the box. It would cover part of the last visible chip row, in a box sized to exactly two rows.
  - (b) A line under the box. It changes the BUILD body height whenever the overflow state flips (a resize), the reflow the fixed box exists to prevent.
  - (c) A CSS-only scroll shadow (background-attachment: local). The chips are opaque and fill the box, so the shade shows only in the 6 px gaps; reasoned, not measured.
- Also pinned (the RM-1767 verifier SHOULD-FIX 1): the BOARD eyebrow is one line at every width with its whole text as the title; that was pinned for the lobby eyebrow only.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_tft_board_more_cue.py`, 16 cases, red 16 of 16 at HEAD: the 1920x920 control (no cue, eyebrow text unchanged, 132 px box); nine sizes 1920 to 480, where the hidden count equals 7 - 2 x columns (an arithmetic expectation, not the code's own method), the cue shows exactly when a chip is below, reads "+N BELOW", is on top at its centre, whole, inside the board, right of the count, 16 px in the eyebrow colour and weight, and the eyebrow row is one line; the companion size reads "+1 BELOW"; 360 and 320 stay inside the box with no sideways scroll (red at 320 with the max-width line removed); at 480 px scrolled one row the cue is still on screen, on top, over the pane colour (red with the sticky lines removed); scrolled to the bottom it hides; a resize 1920 -> 1024 -> 1920 shows then hides it.
- 5-phase audit (headless, tft_round, 1920x920, 1100, 1024, 923x1280, 768, 640, 480, 360, 320; six themes at 923x1280):
  - STRUCTURE: no document x-overflow, the wrapper stays 132 px, no sideways scroll in the board at any size.
  - TYPOGRAPHY: the cue is 16 px computed (`--fs-xs`), no new sub-floor text.
  - HIT-TARGETS: nothing new is clickable; the wrapper keeps its named region and focus ring.
  - ASCII: 0 non-ASCII bytes and 0 CR in the diff.
  - HIERARCHY: unit names stay brightest; the cue matches the eyebrow; it appears only when it has something to say.
- FUTURE: at 360 px the count text keeps only about 7 px (an ellipsis) beside the cue. That window is below every authored size; the count stays in the title.

## 2026-11-06 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 152 (RM-1772)

### RULING

At 720 px and under, the dashboard Active Match view stacks its panes in ONE full-width column: CALL, then BUILD, then the live ARAM balance row (only while it is live), then MAP. Each pane takes its natural height, and the view section (`overflow-y: auto`) scrolls them as one page. 720 px is the line Home (RM-1431) and the header already use: 300% zoom of the 1920x920 baseline is 640 wide, 400% is 480, and WCAG 1.4.10 reflows at 320. Above the line nothing changes (the two columns and the RM-1770 fit-the-window layout). The overlay shell is never affected: every selector is scoped to `body:not([data-shell="overlay"])`.

- Before (measured headless, ui_mock sr / aram / arena / tft): the 1.4fr / 1.6fr columns held at every width: 258 / 294 px at 640, 183 / 209 at 480, 127 / 145 at 360.
  - The BUILD pane content needs 205 px, so at 480 and 360 the pane scrolled SIDEWAYS inside its own vertical scroller (scrollWidth 205 in a clientWidth of 181 / 125). That is two-dimensional scrolling inside a box.
  - The combo action input sat beside the 176 px keystone select at 54 px wide at 640, and 12 px at 480 and 360.
  - At 360 and 320 the view head's sub line (`#am-sub`) ran up to 37 px past the view, because the h2 does not shrink, so the whole section scrolled sideways (scrollWidth 359 in 322).
- In the stacked column:
  - The BUILD pane is not a scroller of its own, because a nested scroller in a scrolling page traps the wheel and the touch drag. It uses `overflow-x: clip` (which keeps its box without making it a scroll container), so its sticky head pins against the view while the page scrolls through it.
  - The view head wraps, so the sub line drops under the title.
  - The combo row wraps, with a 4 px gap in place of the select's left margin. The select drops under the input once the pair no longer fits the input's own width (at 360 and 320). At 640 and 480 they share the line and the input keeps 364 / 204 px.
- Order: CALL first, because the coach call is the most important text. The single column then follows the desktop reading order (left column top to bottom, then the right). MAP is last; it is the least actionable pane without live positions (R30).
- Alternatives:
  - (a) Keep the two columns and drop the RM-1770 fixed height only. The 127 px panes stay too narrow for any of the panes' content.
  - (b) Stack the panes but keep BUILD a fixed-height inner scroller. That makes a nested scroller inside a scrolling page, which traps the wheel and the touch drag.
  - (c) A 900 px line (the PGR loadout one). At 760-900 the two columns measured 314-379 px with no overflow and a 110+ px input, so they still work there.
- Knock-on (this lane's own tests): the RM-1771 "+N BELOW" pinned and scrolled-to-bottom cases used 480 and 640, where the board was one or two columns. In the stacked column the board is the full width (four columns at 640, three at 480), so those cases moved to 760 and 800, which are still two columns. The 320 / 360 never-spill case still passes; the narrow-board ellipsis it was written for is no longer reachable by window size, and its comment says so.
- Also fixed, a U-160 regression in a test: `test_ds_combo_controls_hit_floor.py::test_the_row_is_read_from_one_layout_snapshot` failed run alone since RM-1770 (red with 5fd7df066's active_match.css, green with 5e55f6eb8's and main's). Its 27 px probe pad is an empty flex item in the BUILD pane, which is now a fixed-height flex column whose content overflows, so the default flex-shrink squeezed the pad to 0 px and it moved nothing. The pad is now `flex: none`.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_active_match_narrow_reflow.py`, 22 cases. Red at HEAD: 19 of 22 (the two above-the-line cases and the overlay case pass at HEAD by construction).
  - For ui_mock sr / aram / arena / tft at 640x900, 480x800, 360x740 and 320x640:
    - one grid column, every pane full width, panes in the order CALL / BUILD / MAP with no overlap;
    - no sideways scroll in the document or the view, and no element past the view's content box;
    - the BUILD pane is not a nested scroller;
    - the combo input is at least 160 px, inside its row and at the 42 px floor.
  - A live ARAM balance row sits between BUILD and MAP at 480.
  - The BUILD head is still sticky at the view's padding edge 400 px into the pane (sr / arena, 480).
  - Two columns stay at 722x900 and at the 923x1280 companion size.
  - Under `?overlay=1` at 640 the grid stays `display: block` with the desktop template.
  - Mutations: without the head wrap, 8 red (360 / 320); without the combo-row wrap, 6 red (360 / 320, League modes); without `overflow-y: visible`, 19 red.
- 5-phase audit (headless, the guard's sizes plus 722 and 923x1280):

## 2026-11-06 - lane 4 headless, s144 cycle 153 (RM-1773)

### RULING
- A hover-then-read test waits out the hovered element's transitions, the same as a theme-swap or focus read (RM-1132). A fixed sleep is not a settle: it encodes the transition's duration and goes wrong without failing when that duration changes.
- Found by the RM-1132 inventory (`tests/snapshot_panels/test_theme_swap_reads_settled.py::test_inventory_covers_every_theme_swapping_reader`), which was red on lane/uiux. Two modules were not in it: RM-1763 `test_build_insights_sort_header_hover.py` and RM-1764 `test_last_match_review_nav_hover.py`.
- Audit (headless, all six themes, booted with `?theme=`; the RM-1132 detector plus a no-swap `getAnimations()` sweep right after a real mouse hover and right after un-hover):
  - Build Insights sort header (`.bi-sort-btn`, and the non-sortable "Expected WP" th): nothing transitions on boot, hover or un-hover. No sheet is needed, and none was added.
  - Last Match Review nav tab (`#lm-go-to-review`) and the Graph tab (`.lm-tab`): background-color and color DO transition (`transition: ... 0.12s`) on hover and on un-hover. The module read them after `page.wait_for_timeout(250)`.
- Fix (test only; no page change): `test_last_match_review_nav_hover.py` reads through `_hover` / `_unhover`, which await `getAnimations().finished` on the tab (`_SETTLE`, the `test_champ_select_lock_button_focus` pattern). The two sleeps are gone.
- Alternative: the transitions-off sheet in the opener. Rejected here because awaiting keeps the real 0.12s transition under test, and the pin can then assert the transition still exists (so the verdict is re-audited if it goes away).
- Pin: two new parametrized tests in test_theme_swap_reads_settled.py (6 themes each). The inventory is now 34 modules.
  - The BI one asserts nothing runs after hover or un-hover.
  - The LM one asserts the colour transition runs on the tab itself on a raw hover and a raw un-hover, that `_SETTLE` leaves nothing running after each, that `_hover` / `_unhover` settle, and, by source, that the module has no `wait_for_timeout` and one `page.hover(`.
  - `_SETTLE` awaits only finite running animations (an infinite or paused one never finishes; the sweep after it still sees one) and throws after 20 waits instead of hanging to the pytest timeout (verifier SHOULD-FIX).
- Red and mutations (each restored, sha256-checked):
  - HEAD's inventory: 1 failed.
  - `_SETTLE` made a no-op: the LM module goes 9 of 9 red, so without a settle every read lands mid-transition. The pin goes 6 red.
  - The sleep put back in `_unhover`: the pin goes 6 red.
  - `transition: color 0.3s` added to `.bi-sort-btn`: the BI pin goes 6 red.
- 5-phase audit: no page, CSS or JS change, so STRUCTURE / TYPOGRAPHY / HIT-TARGETS / HIERARCHY are unchanged. ASCII: 0 non-ASCII bytes and 0 CR in the diff.
- FUTURE: the inventory regex only catches modules that change the theme. A hover-then-read module booted on the default theme (no `?theme=`) would not be listed, and nothing would check its settle.

- Fixture finding first: the ui_mock lobby fixture's `mains` and `top8` keys never reach the renderers. `_renderMains` reads `state.latest.lcu.main_champs` (or `/api/mains`), `_top8Load` reads `state.latest.lcu.top8` (or `/api/top8`), and `_lobbyResolveLcu` hands only `phase` / `lobby` / `config` from the fixture. So under plain `?ui_mock=1` both cards show placeholders, and a placeholder-only probe calls them fine. The guard feeds a populated Top 8 and YOUR MAINS through the mock `/api/state` in the `dashboard/routes_lobby_aux.py` response shapes: a 16-character name (the Riot ID name cap), a ranked entry with LP, an unranked one, a tag, five empty slots, four mains with up to three-digit game counts. Synthetic Riot IDs only (RM-906).
- Probe with that data (headless, 722 / 640 / 480 / 360 / 320):
  - TOP 8: the nine-track row shrank every cell. The rank pill showed 93 of its 157 px at 640, 52 at 480, 10 at 320; a 16-character name 12 of 130 px at 320; games, role and tag were cut from 480 down. Nothing reached past the card, so the U-165 containment check stayed green.
  - YOUR MAINS: the FORM cells ran 33 px past their row at 360, and U-165's `min-width: min-content` held the card at 302 px, past the view at 360 and 320.
- Ruling: the U-165 `min-content` hold is removed and a new `@media (max-width: 720px)` block in `web/css/panels/header.css` reflows both cards:
  - A TOP 8 row is two flex lines. Line 1: name (6em basis, grows, may ellipsize past 6em), games, role and rank, none of them cut. A forced break (the row's `::before`, `flex: 0 0 100%`, `order: 1`). Line 2 (`order: 2` from the fifth child on): the tag (4em basis, grows) and the dot, invite, reorder and remove controls at the right edge. A long name pushes the rank onto its own line rather than cutting it. Placeholder rows have the same nine children, so the same positions apply.
  - The search row is a flex row: the input grows from a 6em basis, so the add button stays right-anchored under the invite buttons.
  - A YOUR MAINS row keeps the icon, RECENT and OVERALL on line 1 (`76px minmax(0, 1fr) minmax(0, 1fr)`) and puts FORM under them (`grid-column: 2 / -1`). The FORM header is hidden there, since each FORM value carries its own KP% / CS/m / Dmg label.
  - At 400 px and under the copy button sits under the icon (a 50 px column): with 76 px, RECENT and OVERALL got 53 px each at 320, which cut "12/4/8" by a pixel and pushed the OVERALL header 10 px past its row.
  - A `margin-inline: 2px` on the TOP 8 dot was tried and dropped: the one-rule mutation passed, because the controls are right-anchored.
- Scope line: the PARTY card's duo-synergy win-rate cells (RM-881, the queue lane's) overflow their 0 px boxes at 360 and under, so the view still scrolls sideways there. Pinned as strict xfails, so that fix turns the file red until the marks are lifted. PARTY MAINS was not probed with data (its renderer reads `lcu.lobby` mastery the guard does not feed).
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_lobby_mains_top8_narrow_reflow.py`, 41 cases (39 pass + 2 strict xfail). At HEAD's CSS 20 failed.
- Mutations (each restored, sha256-checked):
  - No forced row break: 3 failed.
  - No TOP 8 flex: 8 failed.
  - No FORM span: 4 failed.
  - No 400 px block: 5 failed.
  - Name basis `0` instead of 6em: 1 failed.
- 5-phase audit (headless):
  - STRUCTURE: MAINS and TOP 8 inside the view at 320-640; no row scrolls sideways; the view stops scrolling sideways at 640 and 480; nothing changes above 720 (722 keeps the nine-track TOP 8 grid, the four-track MAINS grid and the FORM header, pinned).
  - TYPOGRAPHY: no size changed.
  - HIT-TARGETS: invite / remove stay 26x26, the search input 42 px tall, the stacked copy button 22x22; no TOP 8 hit overlay takes the centre of a name, games, role or rank cell (elementFromPoint, pinned).
  - ASCII: 0 non-ASCII bytes and 0 CR in the diff.
  - HIERARCHY: element captures of both cards at 480 and 320 (headless, `_scratch/`, not committed): the name is still the first and brightest item of a TOP 8 row, the tag and controls sit on the second line; in MAINS the result and W-L colours lead, FORM reads as a sub-row.
- FUTURE: the ui_mock lobby fixture's `mains` / `top8` keys are dead (wire them through `_lobbyResolveLcu`, or drop them); the YOUR MAINS copy button is 20 px wide, not 22, at every width above 400 (desktop included: its 76 px column holds 50 + 6 + 22 = 78 px); at 320 OVERALL stacks six short lines ("124 / Games / 68W - / 56L / 3.66 / KDA"); TOP 8 placeholder rows are two lines tall under 720; the desktop unranked cell clips "LVL 412 : Unranked" by 4 px at 722; the Lobby still scrolls sideways from 721 to about 800 px (the 720 line); PARTY MAINS narrow with data.

Each table broke out of its pane below about 825 px (Runes; Items 805, Skills 802, Spells 686: table width plus the 58 px insets on each side), and the view itself scrolled sideways below about 786 px (Runes; Items 766, Skills 763, Spells 647; derived from the measured section widths at 722, 728 / 725 / 748 / 609 px in a 684 px box, not measured at each width). At 320 the Items table ran about 445 px past the view. The Benchmarks tab already scrolled its table inside `.cb-table-wrap`.
- Ruling: a WPA table is the WCAG 1.4.10 data-table exception, so it may scroll sideways, but inside its own mount, never the view.

  - `web/js/panels/build_insights.js` `_revealInMount`: Chrome's focus scroll leaves a partly visible target where it is, so the Win Rate sort button sat 5 to 56 px past the mount's edge after Tab, and a re-sort (Enter) widened it by its arrow without scrolling. A `focus` listener on each sort button scrolls ONLY the mount, only sideways, just far enough to show the whole button (left or right); the page never moves. The U-151 restore re-focuses the new button after a re-sort, so the same listener covers it.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_build_insights_narrow_table_scroll.py`, 171 cases (four tabs x 800 / 722 / 640 / 480 / 360 / 320: the view and page do not scroll sideways, the mount lies in its pane and is the scroller with no vertical scroll, the Win Rate column is reachable, the focused Win Rate / WPA sort button is wholly visible after focus, re-sort and a back-focus from the far end; plus 1920 / 1280 / 1000 pins). At HEAD's CSS and JS 65 failed.
- Mutations (each restored, sha256-checked): no `overflow-x` 120 failed; no `max-width` 41 failed; no focus listener 32 failed; no left-scroll branch 4 failed. A `Math.min` clamp for a button wider than the mount passed its mutation (no such button down to 320), so it was dropped.
- 5-phase audit (headless):
  - STRUCTURE: the four WPA tabs no longer scroll the view at 320-800; the mount is the only sideways scroller and has no vertical scroll; nothing moves at 1000 / 1280 / 1920.
  - TYPOGRAPHY: no size changed (sort button 16 px, numeric cells 22 px at 1920 and 320).
  - HIT-TARGETS: sort buttons stay 42 px tall; the focus ring is the inset box-shadow, so the mount's clip cannot cut it; every sort button is reachable by keyboard and shown whole when focused.
  - ASCII: 0 non-ASCII bytes and 0 CR in every touched file.
  - HIERARCHY: Items pane captures at 1920 / 640 / 480 / 320 (headless, `_scratch/`, not committed): the TAKEAWAY rail leads above the table under 1200, the table's cut right edge reads as "more this way".
- FUTURE: the chart tabs still push the view at 360 / 320 (Game Length `.dw-champ` clear button at 360 and 320; the `.dw-modes` / `.pf-modes` / `.op-modes` / `.cb-modes` button rows and Snowball `.se-val` at 320, 13-56 px); headless Chromium draws overlay scrollbars, so the classic scrollbar a Windows Chrome / Electron window shows under a scrolling table was not measured (its height adds to the mount, no vertical scroll expected).

- Measured before (headless Chromium, `?ui_mock=1#lobby` with four synthetic mains): the 76 px icon column held `.lv-mc-champ` = 50 px icon + 6 px gap + 22 px button = 78 px, so the button (the one flex child free to shrink; the icon is `flex-shrink: 0`) drew 20 x 22 at 1920x920, 1280, 722, 640 and 480 on YOUR MAINS, and on PARTY MAINS at every width including 400 and 320 (PARTY MAINS never stacks the button under the icon; only YOUR MAINS does, at 400 and under, where it already kept 22 px).
- Ruling: `.lv-mc-champ { gap: 6px 4px }` - the column gap is 4 px, so 50 + 4 + 22 = 76 fits the column the original E11 comment sized for "icon + copy button"; the row gap stays 6 px, so the stacked YOUR MAINS cell at 400 and under is unchanged. `.lv-mc-copy` gets `flex-shrink: 0` so a future squeeze overflows visibly instead of silently shrinking the box. No column track changed, so no header / cell alignment moved.
- After (measured): 22 x 22 at all seven widths on both tabs; the button inside its champ cell and the cell inside its row; the icon 50 x 50; beside the icon everywhere except YOUR MAINS at 400 and under (stacked).
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_lobby_mains_copy_box.py` (3 tests x 7 widths x 2 tabs = 42 cases; as committed, 12 fail at HEAD's CSS, all in `test_copy_button_keeps_its_22px_box`: five widths above 400 on both tabs plus PARTY MAINS at 400 and 320; the draft also failed 2 icon / button cases from a wrong assumption that PARTY MAINS stacks, corrected before the fix). Verifier (independent, read-only): REFUTE on that count only (it read 14, now corrected), fix and guard confirmed; 256 passed, 3 xfailed, rc 0 over the guard and 8 neighbours; restore checked by sha256. NICE-TO-HAVE not taken: pin the PARTY MAINS row count. `test_lobby_mains_top8_narrow_reflow.py` no longer encodes the 20 px squeeze (it asserted >= 20 above 400; now == 22 everywhere).
- Live-half digest: `tests/guard_data/web_live_half/css_header.txt` and `css_panels.txt` re-captured. The guard was already red on lane/uiux from cycle 166 (S144-U-176 edited base / header / input_activity / primitives / replay_events CSS without a re-capture); this re-capture covers those five plus this cycle's header.css edit.
- 5-phase audit (headless): STRUCTURE - button inside its cell at every width, no row scrolls (narrow reflow guard green); TYPOGRAPHY - no size changed; HIT-TARGETS - the 44 px ::before overlay is unchanged and now centred on a 22 px box (`test_lobby_hit_targets.py` green); ASCII - 0 non-ASCII bytes added, LF only; HIERARCHY - unchanged (the button is 2 px wider, the icon unchanged).
- FUTURE: PARTY MAINS at 400 and under keeps the 76 px icon column (the U-166 400 px stack is YOUR MAINS only); its Player / Mastery / Recent cells at 320 were not measured for cuts this cycle.

### S144-U-178 - Settings checkboxes keep their 24 px square at every width (s144 uiux cycle 168)

- Row: the U-169 FUTURE "the API SPEND GATES Dev mode checkbox draws smaller than the gate checkboxes below it" (seen in a capture, not measured).
- Before (measured, headless Chromium, `/?ui_mock=1#settings`, Dev mode on, two synthetic gates): every `.settings-row` checkbox is a flex item with the default `flex-shrink: 1` beside a text span that breaks between words, so from 720 px down the box gave its width to the text. 720: Dev mode 19.4 x 24, Force Flash on Snowball 20 x 24, Dev UI mock data 21 x 24, push spells 22.9 x 24. 480: eight rows narrowed, Dev mode / Dev UI mock / Force Flash 13 x 24. 320: all eight long-label rows 13 x 24. 1920 and 923: all 27 boxes 24 x 24. The spend-gate rows and the PANEL VISIBILITY cells kept 24 x 24 at every width (short labels), which is why Dev mode read smaller than the gates under it.
- Ruling: the visible control keeps its declared square at every width; the label text wraps instead. `.settings-row input[type="checkbox"] { flex: none }` (`web/css/panels/header.css`).
- After (measured): 27 of 27 checkboxes 24 x 24 at 1920, 923, 720, 480, 400 and 320; no `label.settings-row` scrolls sideways and the document is never wider than the viewport at 1920 / 720 / 480 / 320; the Dev mode row grows 50 -> 77 / 103 / 156 px at 720 / 480 / 320 as its text wraps.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_settings_checkbox_hit_targets.py` `test_every_settings_checkbox_keeps_its_square_box_at_every_width` (5 widths; 3 fail at HEAD's CSS - 720, 480, 320 - and pass with the fix). Live-half digest `tests/guard_data/web_live_half/css_header.txt` re-captured (the only area that moved).
- 5-phase audit (headless): STRUCTURE - no row or document overflow at any measured width; TYPOGRAPHY - unchanged (label 22 px); HIT-TARGETS - the label stays the target (the existing label-click, hit-min and focus-ring tests green at 1920 / 923); ASCII - 0 non-ASCII bytes added, LF only; HIERARCHY - unchanged, the boxes now read as one control family.

### S144-U-179 - the Replay meta line and match rows fit the view under the 320 floor, and a long match id breaks inside the pane at every width (s144 uiux cycle 169)

- Row: the U-175 FUTURE "At 300 px, #replay-meta runs past the view".
- Before (measured, headless Chromium, `/?ui_mock=1#replay`): `.replay-meta` is `flex: 1` with the default `min-width: auto`, so it never shrank below its unbroken match id. 300 px, first match loaded: the 191 px id `MOCK_NORMAL_DRAFT_WIN` ran 66 -> 257 in a pane whose content ends at 248 (and past the view), and the narrow pane's `overflow-x: clip` cut the id off. The long-id ui_mock match (`MOCK_ARENA_LOSS_OVERFLOW_LONG_NAME_FOR_ELLIPSIS_TEST`, 473 px) ran past the pane at 860, 722, 480, 360 and 320 too; the U-175 guard loaded the first match only, so it never saw it. From 280 down each match row's queue label (`.replay-match-queue`, nowrap flex line) ran past its row and the view beside the champion name; at 300 the NORMAL DRAFT / RANKED SOLO labels broke inside themselves into 38 px two-line boxes.
- Ruling: the meta wants 12rem on the clock's line (the clock still drops under it where both do not fit; for the first ui_mock match that is the same threshold as before, the old min-content being its 191 px id, while for the long id and the resting prompt the clock now drops at 12rem of meta width rather than at the longest word, not measured at rest), may shrink to the pane (`min-width: 0`) and breaks an id longer than the pane (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`); the match row's top line wraps (`flex-wrap: wrap`), so a queue label that does not fit drops whole to its own line, still right-aligned by its auto margin. `web/css/panels/primitives.css` `.replay-meta`, `.replay-match-top`.
- After (measured): nothing past the view at 300 / 280 at rest, loaded and with the long id loaded; the meta and clock inside the main pane there and at 860 / 722 / 640 / 480 / 360 / 320 with the long id; every match row's top line inside its content box with no part over one line. Unchanged: 1920 / 1280 / 640 (first match) meta and clock boxes identical before and after; 1920 meta one line, clock beside it. Rows at 300 grow 83 / 101 -> 110 px where the queue label drops (all above --hit-min).
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_replay_narrow_reflow.py` (`test_under_the_floor_*` at 300x640 / 280x640, `test_the_long_match_id_breaks_inside_the_pane` at six widths, `test_the_baseline_meta_is_one_line_beside_the_clock`); 20 fail at HEAD's CSS, 72 pass with the fix. Live-half digest `tests/guard_data/web_live_half/css_panels.txt` re-captured (the only area that moved).
- 5-phase audit (headless): STRUCTURE - no view or document overflow at any measured width; TYPOGRAPHY - no size changed; HIT-TARGETS - match rows 83 to 129 px tall, all above 42, still the same buttons; ASCII - 0 non-ASCII bytes added, LF only; HIERARCHY - unchanged (the meta stays dim, the clock stays the accent stat).
- FUTURE: a real match id (`<platform>_<digits>`, about 14 characters) is far shorter than the ui_mock ids, so the long-id case is the fixture's adversarial row, not live data; nothing under 280 px is pinned (240 px was probed before the fix only, where three queue labels ran past the view).

MASTERY takes 1.15 of RECENT's share, because at 320 the 130 px the two get must hold "Mastery" (67 px) and the RECENT header (57 px). At 400 and under the copy button sits under the icon (50 px column), as on YOUR MAINS. `web/css/panels/header.css`, the S144-U-180 blocks after the RM-1776 ones. Default taken (no operator): PLAYER moves to the second line rather than MASTERY, so the headers that stay visible keep sitting over their cells.

### S144-U-181 - every Post Game Review tab label clears its border (s144 uiux cycle 171)

- Row: the U-172 FUTURE "`.lm-tab` has 0 px side padding, so AI ANALYSIS touches its active border" (carried since cycle 162).
- Before (measured, headless Chromium, `/?ui_mock=1&mode=<sr|aram|arena>#last-match`, 1920 to 320): `.lm-tab` was `padding: 8px 0` with a 96 px min-width, so a label wider than the min-width set the tab's width itself: AI Analysis and Review had 0.0 px between their ink box and the 1 px border on both sides (the active AI Analysis label sat flush on its accent border); Graph had 10.9, Build 15.9 (min-width slack only). Same numbers in all three modes and at every width. A CSS scan of every bordered `cursor: pointer` rule in `web/css` for a 0 side padding found `.lm-tab` the only one.
- Ruling: `.lm-tab` gets `padding: 8px var(--space-2)` (8 px). Not `--space-3` (12 px): measured, that makes the one-line strip 497 px, 1 px past the 496 px nav at a 640 px window, so Review would drop to a second line there; at 8 px every pinned width keeps the line structure U-172 pinned (one line at 1920 / 722 / 640, the same wraps at 560 and under).
- After (measured): AI Analysis and Review 8.0 / 8.0, Graph 10.9, Build 15.9 at every width in all three modes. One-line geometry at 1920 / 722 / 640: lefts 0 / 151.5 / 253.5 / 355.5, widths 145.5 / 96 / 96 / 123.5 (were 0 / 136 / 238 / 340, 130 / 96 / 96 / 108).
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_last_match_tabs_narrow.py` `test_label_clears_its_border` (24 cases; all 24 fail at HEAD's CSS, and all 24 under a `padding: 8px 4px` mutation); the file's pinned wide geometry was re-pinned to the new widths (33 of 240 fail at HEAD's CSS, 240 pass with the fix).
- 5-phase audit (headless): STRUCTURE - every tab inside the nav's content box, the strip never scrolls sideways, Review still ends a wrapped line at the nav's right edge; TYPOGRAPHY - no size changed (--fs-sm), padding on a token; HIT-TARGETS - tabs 42 px tall (the floor), 96 px or wider, the 2 px focus ring at 2 px offset inside the tabbed section at every width; ASCII - 0 non-ASCII bytes, LF; HIERARCHY - no colour changed, the active tab's accent border now frames its label instead of touching it.
- FUTURE: the labels are uppercase with 0.08em letter-spacing, so each ink box carries the trailing spacing after its last letter (about 1.4 px), and the label sits that much left of true centre; not measured as visible at 1920.

### S144-U-182 - a Replay pick brings the loaded match into view where the panes stack (s144 uiux cycle 172)

- Row: the U-175 FUTURE "Picking a row at 720 and under does not bring the main pane into view (the list sits above it; JS, not taken)".
- Before (measured, headless Chromium, `/?ui_mock=1#replay` with the ui_mock replay padded to 12 matches, the third row brought into sight and picked; "view" = the view section's visible box): 640x307 (300% zoom of the 1920x920 baseline) view 99-265, main pane from 318 (53 px under the view); 480x230 view 99-188, main pane from 280; 320x256 view 99-214, main pane from 245; 320x640 view 99-598, meta row 584-696 (14 px showing) and the scrubber 714-758 under the view; 640x900 meta 538-589 and scrubber 607-651, already showing. The PGR "Review ->" arrival scrolled only the picked row into view, so its match stayed below it too.
- Ruling: once a picked match has loaded (the meta line can grow a line on load), `_replayRevealMain` (`web/js/panels/dev.js`) scrolls the least that shows the main pane's head - its top edge through the bottom of `#replay-slider`; where that head is taller than the view, the main pane's top leads (the meta line shows). It runs for a click, an Enter / Space on a row and a PGR arrival, only for the pick still marked active, only where the panes stack (main pane top at or under the list's bottom, so the overlay shell and every side-by-side width are untouched), and only when the head is not already showing. Focus is NOT moved: the picked row keeps it, so Tab and Shift+Tab walk the list as before.
- Alternatives: (a) move focus to the scrubber or the main pane on a pick - rejected, a keyboard user walking the list would lose their place on every pick, and focus moves on activation are the surprising half of WCAG 3.2; (b) `main.scrollIntoView({block: "start"})` on every pick - rejected, it also scrolls where the head already shows (640x900) and hides the list more than needed; (c) reveal at pick time, before the load - rejected, measured: the meta line grows from 31 to 51 px (640) once the match text lands (640x900, measured), so a reveal sized on the loading line would leave the scrubber up to 20 px short (inferred from that growth; a pre-load variant was not run).
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_replay_pick_reveals_main.py`, 13 tests: click at 640x307 / 480x230 / 320x256 / 320x640, Enter and Space at 640x307 / 320x640 and a PGR arrival (sessionStorage `rc-replay-focus-match`) at 640x307 / 320x256 show the head (or lead with the main pane's top where the head is taller than the view), and focus stays on the picked row; control 640x900, the view does not move; controls 722x400 and 1920x920 (side by side), neither the view nor the main pane's own scroll moves. Red against HEAD's dev.js (the first 11-test version, Enter only): 8 failed, 3 passed (the controls). The live-half digest row for dev.js (js_panels.txt) is re-captured (code changed, no glyph).
- 5-phase audit (headless): STRUCTURE - no CSS or DOM change, the stacked and side-by-side layouts are those of RM-1785 (test_replay_narrow_reflow.py green); TYPOGRAPHY - none changed; HIT-TARGETS - no new control, the row stays the focused control after a pick by key; ASCII - 0 non-ASCII bytes, LF; HIERARCHY - after a pick the loaded match's meta line and scrubber are what the view shows, not the list that was just used.
- FUTURE: the picked row leaves the view on a short window, so its focus ring is off-screen until the next Tab (the browser scrolls a newly focused row back); the scoreboard and Timeline under the scrubber still need a scroll, by design (the head is the minimum). Pre-existing (verifier): if pick A's load resolves after pick B's, A's data still replaces `_REPLAY.match` and the meta line while B stays marked active (`_replayLoadMatch` has no stale-response check); the reveal is skipped for A, the data race is not fixed here.

### S144-U-183 - a late reply for an earlier Replay pick no longer paints over the later pick (s144 uiux cycle 173)

- Row: the U-182 FUTURE (verifier) "if pick A's load resolves after pick B's, A's data still replaces `_REPLAY.match` and the meta line while B stays marked active".
- Before (measured, headless Chromium, live `/api` path with replies routed from the ui_mock replay fixture, 1920x920; A = MOCK_NORMAL_DRAFT_WIN with its match and events replies held, B = the Arena row): pick A, pick B, B loads; release A and the meta line reads "MOCK_NORMAL_DRAFT_WIN - Normal Draft - 35:23 - patch 16.10 (W)" under row B, which stays the active, aria-current row. With only the events half unguarded the ribbon reads A's 15 events under B's meta (B has 3). Read from the code, not separately measured: the late A reply also set the scrubber range, minimap and grid to A, and called `loadReviewPlayer(A, team)`, which (its match id now B) reset the recording lane to A and fetched `/api/recordings/A` again.
- Ruling: only the newest request's reply is applied. `_replayLoadMatch` (`web/js/panels/dev.js`) takes `seq = ++_REPLAY.loadSeq` per pick and drops a reply whose seq is no longer current; `loadReplayEvents` (`web/js/panels/replay_events.js`) does the same with `_RE.seq` on its live and ui_mock paths, success and failure alike, so a late reply for an earlier pick or an earlier timeline filter (items / skills / wards re-call it on the same match) cannot replace the newer ribbon. `review_player.js` already carried its own `_RP.seq` guard.
- Alternatives: (a) compare the reply's `match_id` with the active row - rejected, A -> B -> A would accept the first A reply after the second A pick, and a filter change on one match would still race; (b) `AbortController` on the earlier fetch - rejected, the ui_mock path shares one cached promise across all picks (aborting it would break later picks), and a seq check is enough to make a late reply inert.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_replay_stale_load.py`, 2 tests: A held, B picked and loaded, A released, then the meta line, scrubber max, grid frame 0 champions, event count and ribbon rows are B's, row B is the only active / aria-current row, and `/api/recordings/A` is not fetched again; control, B picked alone loads exactly that state. Red against HEAD: 1 failed (meta names A), 1 passed (control). Mutations: the dev.js guard removed - fails on the meta line; the events guard removed (`fresh` always true) - fails on the event count (15, want 3). The live-half digest rows for dev.js and replay_events.js (js_panels.txt) are re-captured (code changed, no glyph).
- 5-phase audit (headless): STRUCTURE - no CSS or DOM change; TYPOGRAPHY - none changed; HIT-TARGETS - no new control, the pick rows unchanged (test_replay_match_row_keyboard.py green); ASCII - 0 non-ASCII bytes, LF; HIERARCHY - the highlighted row, the meta line and the data under it now always name the same match.
- FUTURE: when the NEWEST pick's reply fails (`null` or a rejected fetch) the meta line stays "loading <id>..." and the grid keeps the previous match; a "could not load" state is not built here.

### S144-U-184 - a Replay pick that fails, or has no timeline frames, says so and drops the previous match (s144 uiux cycle 174)

- Row: the U-183 FUTURE "when the NEWEST pick's reply fails (`null` or a rejected fetch) the meta line stays 'loading <id>...' and the grid keeps the previous match".
- Before (measured, headless Chromium, live `/api` path with replies routed from the ui_mock replay fixture, 1920x920; A = MOCK_NORMAL_DRAFT_WIN, B = MOCK_RANKED_SOLO_WIN_ZERO_KDA): pick A, then pick B with a 404 or an aborted fetch - the meta line reads "loading B..." for good (the wait for any other text times out) while the scrubber, clock, minimap and grid keep A under the highlighted row B. Sibling, same root (the reply handler only ever painted on success): B loaded with `snapshots: []` set the meta line to B but `_replayRenderSnapshot` returned early, so A's ten grid rows and clock stayed under it. The real store holds such matches: `data/rewind_history.db` (read-only), 4 match rows with no `timeline_frames` (queue 2400 x1, 830 x1, null queue x2).
- Second defect found by the audit, pre-existing: the grid placeholder `<td colspan="7" class="home-empty">` computed `display: flex` (the shared `.replay-grid .home-empty` rule), which takes the cell out of table layout, so the colspan was ignored - the page-open "no match selected" note sat in a 105 px column at the grid's left edge, 153 px tall (span 0.054 of the header row). MUST-FIX in the same slice because the new notes use the same cell.
- Ruling: in `_replayLoadMatch` (`web/js/panels/dev.js`) a null or rejected reply for the newest pick clears `_REPLAY.match`, disables the scrubber at max 0, sets the meta line to "could not load <id> - pick it again to retry" (no raw error text, CLAUDE.md error-handling rule) and shows the frame-less state; a loaded match with no frames shows that state with "no timeline frames stored for this match". The frame-less state (`_replayShowNoFrames`) sets the clock to "-", hides the minimap and leaves one placeholder row in the grid, the same cell the page opens with. Row B stays the active pick so the retry is one click or key. `web/css/panels/primitives.css`: `.replay-grid td.home-empty` is `display: table-cell` again, sigil a block above the centred note.
- Alternatives: (a) keep the previous match visible with a "could not load" banner - rejected, the highlighted row, meta line and data would name different matches again (the U-183 contract); (b) retry automatically - rejected, a 404 does not heal by itself and a loop would hammer the route; (c) drop `.home-empty` from the grid cell and add a new class - rejected, one scoped display override keeps the shared empty-state look.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_replay_failed_load.py`, 5 tests: B fails by 404 and by abort (meta text, grid holds only the "match did not load" row, clock "-", scrubber disabled at max 0, minimap hidden, row B active, re-pick loads B); B with no frames (grid holds only the "no timeline frames" row, same A-free checks); the page-open placeholder; control A alone. Each placeholder cell must compute `table-cell` and span more than 95% of the header row. Red against HEAD: 3 failed, 1 passed (control) before the span test existed; with the dev.js fix and without the CSS fix: 4 failed (span 0.054), 1 passed. Live-half digest rows re-captured for dev.js (js_panels.txt) and primitives.css (css_panels.txt) - code changed, no glyph.
- 5-phase audit (headless, 1920x920 / 640x307 / 320x600): STRUCTURE - no horizontal overflow on the document or the view section; the meta line stays inside the main pane and wraps at 640 and 320; the placeholder spans the grid. TYPOGRAPHY - meta 18 px, note 18 px (`--fs-sm`), both on tokens. HIT-TARGETS - no new control; focus stays on the picked row after a failure. ASCII - 0 non-ASCII bytes, LF. HIERARCHY - meta line 7.50:1 and the note 6.31:1, each against the main pane background oklch(0.22 0.05 300); the failure is named in the line that names the match.
- FUTURE: a frame with zero `entries` (the ui_mock ARAM row) still renders an empty grid body with no note; the Timeline ribbon of a failed pick can still list its events (the events route is separate), which is accurate but sits under a "could not load" meta line.

### S144-U-185 - a Replay frame with no player rows says so, and every grid note stays on screen down to 320 px (s144 uiux cycle 175)

- Row: the U-184 FUTURE "a frame with zero `entries` (the ui_mock ARAM row) still renders an empty grid body with no note".
- Before (measured, headless Chromium, `?ui_mock=1#replay`, 1920x920): pick MOCK_ARAM_NEUTRAL (one frame, `entries: []`) - the meta line names the match and the clock reads "t = 0.0min" while `#replay-grid-body` holds no row at all: a header over blank space, the same look as a view that has not painted. Same root on the live path: a match whose frame N has no entries left that frame blank on a scrub. Reach: `core/replay_history.py` builds one entry per stored participant for every frame, so an entry-less frame is a match with frames but no participant rows; `data/rewind_history.db` (read-only) holds 0 such matches today (2966 match rows), so the live reach is the ui_mock fixture and any future partial ingest.
- Second defect found by the audit, pre-existing: at 480 px and under the scoreboard keeps its 414 px table and scrolls sideways inside `.replay-grid-wrap` (RM-1777), and the note was centred across the whole table - at 480 the new note ran 23 px past the 348 px pane, at 360 / 320 the page-open "no match selected" ran 56 / 96 px past the 228 / 188 px pane, the sigil off-view too. MUST-FIX in the same slice because the new note uses the same cell.
- Ruling: `_replayRenderSnapshot` (`web/js/panels/dev.js`) shows one note row "no player rows stored for this frame" for an entry-less frame; the note row builder is shared (`_replayGridNote`, also used by `_replayShowNoFrames`). The note text sits in a `<span class="replay-grid-note">` (the page-open placeholder in `web/index.html` too): a sticky box pinned to the pane's left edge, `100cqw` of the wrap wide (`.replay-grid-wrap` is now an inline-size container), carrying the sigil, so note and sigil stay centred in what is on screen; the cell keeps its vertical padding and `display: table-cell` (U-184).
- Alternatives: (a) hide the grid table on an empty frame - rejected, the header is the column legend and its height would reflow under the scrubber on every scrub (no-reflow rule); (b) let the note wrap at a fixed max-width - rejected, the cell is still centred across the 414 px table, so the box stays partly off-view at 320; (c) a JS width read on resize - rejected, a container query unit does it with no listener.

Red against HEAD: test 1 (empty grid) and test 2 (empty grid on frame 1); with the dev.js note and without the span / CSS half test 3 fails at 480 ("no match selected" 66 px off centre in the 348 px pane). Live-half digest rows re-captured: js_panels.txt (dev.js), css_panels.txt (primitives.css), web_other.txt (index.html) - code changed, no glyph.

### S144-U-186 - a Replay pick that fails lists no Timeline events (s144 uiux cycle 176)

- Defect (the U-184 FUTURE row): the Timeline ribbon (`web/js/panels/replay_events.js`) and the match reply (`web/js/panels/dev.js` `_replayLoadMatch`) are two fetches from two routes. When the match reply failed (404, 5xx, a dropped connection) and the events route answered, the ribbon listed the failed pick's events under "could not load <id>", each a "Jump the scrubber to m:ss" button whose click did nothing (`_replaySeekToClock` returns with no match loaded), and an items / skills / wards toggle re-fetched events for the match that did not load. If the events reply was still pending at the failure, the ribbon kept the PREVIOUS pick's rows under the failed pick until it landed.
- Ruling: a failed pick clears the ribbon. New export `clearReplayEventsFailed()` (called from `failed()` in `_replayLoadMatch`) bumps the ribbon's request seq (a late events reply for that pick is dropped), forgets the match id (a filter toggle has nothing to re-fetch), empties rows and flags, and sets the note to "No timeline events - the match did not load."; the count reads 0. The section stays shown (no reflow under the meta line). The next `loadReplayEvents` call restores the markup's plain note "No timeline events for this match." (`_renderEvents` now writes the note text every render).
- Alternatives: (a) hide the section on failure (the module header's "hidden until _replayLoadMatch resolves") - rejected, the section collapses and reflows the pane under the meta line on every failed pick (no-reflow rule) and the operator loses the place where events will reappear on retry; (b) keep the rows but make them inert - rejected, rows of a match that did not load are not data the operator can use, and a list of dead buttons is a hit-target lie; (c) clear the ribbon at the START of every pick - out of scope here (a flash on every successful pick), filed FUTURE below.
- Frameless matches (U-184 second case) are left as they are: in the real store all 4 matches with no frames also have 0 timeline events (read-only DB read, 2966 matches), so no event row can appear without a frame to seek.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_replay_failed_load_ribbon.py`, 4 tests: B's match reply 404 / aborts while its events answer (0 rows, count 0, the failed note shown; picking B again lists its 3 events and the plain note is back); B's events reply held until after the failure (still 0 rows); control A lists its 15 events. Red against HEAD: 3 failed (3 rows listed under the failed pick), control passed. Live-half digest rows re-captured: js_panels.txt (dev.js, replay_events.js) - code changed, no glyph.
- 5-phase audit (headless, 1920x920 / 720 / 320, failed state): STRUCTURE - no document horizontal overflow (scrollWidth equals the viewport at all three); the note box sits inside the section at every width (15 px inset each side; at 320 the text wraps inside the 158 px box). TYPOGRAPHY - note 18 px (`--fs-sm`), count 16 px, both unchanged rules. HIT-TARGETS - no focusable element added; the dead row buttons are gone. ASCII - 0 non-ASCII bytes, LF, in all touched files. HIERARCHY - the note `--text-faint` oklch(0.63 0.055 305) is 4.91:1 against the pane background oklch(0.22 0.05 300) (AA), the same pair the plain note already used.
- FUTURE: while a new pick is loading, the ribbon still lists the previous pick's rows under "loading <id>..." and a click seeks the previous match.

### S144-U-187 - a Replay pick that is still loading lists none of the previous pick's Timeline events, and a ribbon row never seeks another match (s144 uiux cycle 177)

- Row: the U-186 FUTURE "while a new pick is loading, the ribbon still lists the previous pick's rows under 'loading <id>...', and a click seeks the previous match".
- Before (measured, headless Chromium, live `/api` path with replies routed from the ui_mock replay fixture, 1920x920; A = MOCK_NORMAL_DRAFT_WIN, 15 events, frames 0/10/20/30 min; B = the Arena row, 3 events, frames 0/5/10 min): pick A, then pick B with B's match and events replies held - the ribbon kept A's 15 rows and count 15 under "loading B...". Second case, same root (the ribbon and the scrubber are fed by two routes): B's events landed before B's match - the ribbon listed B's rows while the scrubber still held A, and a click on B's 7:52 row moved A's scrubber to A's 10 min frame (slider 0 -> 1) under a meta line naming B.
- Ruling: `loadReplayEvents` (`web/js/panels/replay_events.js`) for a DIFFERENT match than the ribbon holds drops the rows and flags at once, shows "Loading timeline events...", sets the count to "-" (no data yet, not 0) and marks the list `aria-busy`; the reply clears all three. The section keeps its height (an inline `min-height` read before the clear, released on the reply's render), so the grid under it moves once (A's height to B's), as before, never twice. `.replay-events-empty` (`web/css/panels/replay_events.css`) is `flex: 1 0 auto`, so the note sits centred in the held box; inert in every other state (the section is content-height, `.replay-map-row` aligns flex-start). A filter toggle on the same match keeps its rows. A row click now passes the ribbon's match id to the seek handler, and `_replaySeekToClock` (`web/js/panels/dev.js`) ignores a row whose match is not the one in the scrubber; once B's match loads, the same row seeks B.
- Alternatives: (a) keep A's rows but disable them while B loads - rejected, the ribbon still names A's events under "loading B..." (the defect) and disabling a focused row throws focus to `<body>`; (b) hold B's rows until B's match reply lands - rejected, couples the two routes' render order and hides data that is already right; (c) clear without holding the height - rejected, the grid would move twice per pick (up on the clear, down on the reply), the reflow U-186 alternative (c) feared; (d) queue a click made before B loads and apply it on load - rejected, a seek that fires seconds after the click is a surprise, not navigation.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_replay_pending_pick_ribbon.py`, 3 tests: both B replies held (0 rows, count "-", the loading note shown and filling the held box within 1 px, `aria-busy`, section height equal to A's; B's rows once its events land, no note or `aria-busy` left); B's events in, match held (a click on B's 7:52 row leaves A's slider, clock and grid as they were; after B loads the row seeks B's frame 2); control: an items toggle on A with its re-fetch held keeps A's 15 rows, and A's own 10:12 row seeks frame 1. Red against HEAD: 2 failed (15 rows under "loading B"; slider 0 -> 1), control passed. The fill assertion alone, without the CSS line: 1 failed (213 px of blank under the note). Live-half digest rows re-captured: js_panels.txt (dev.js, replay_events.js), css_panels.txt (replay_events.css) - code changed, no glyph.
- 5-phase audit (headless, loading state, 1920x920 / 720x900 / 320x640): STRUCTURE - no document horizontal overflow (scrollWidth equals the viewport at all three); the note box sits 15 px inside the section each side and fills it to the bottom padding (section 409 / 409 / 557 px held). TYPOGRAPHY - note 18 px (`--fs-sm`), count 16 px, unchanged rules. HIT-TARGETS - no focusable element added; focus stays on the picked match row; the stale seek buttons are gone. ASCII - 0 non-ASCII bytes, LF, in all touched files. HIERARCHY - the note rgb(145,129,164) is 4.92:1 and the "-" count rgb(177,164,194) 7.50:1, each against the section background oklch(0.22 0.05 300) (AA).
- FUTURE: B's rows that land before B's match are listed but a click on one does nothing until the match loads (no affordance says so); the scrubber, clock and grid still show A under "loading B..." until B's match lands (the U-183 design; a matching "loading" state for the grid is unscoped).

### S144-U-188 - a Replay Timeline row that cannot seek is a disabled button, and the ribbon says why (s144 uiux cycle 178)

- Defect (the U-187 FUTURE row): pick A, then pick B. When B's events reply landed before B's match reply, B's rows were listed with every live-control affordance (pointer cursor, hover fill, a tab stop, "Jump the scrubber to 7:52") while the scrubber still held A; since U-187 a click on one correctly did nothing, but nothing on screen said so. Sibling: a match stored with no timeline frames (U-184) lists its rows the same way, and `_replaySeekToClock` returns early on no snapshots, so every row there was a dead control too.
- Ruling: `replay_events.js` keeps the match the scrubber holds and its frame count, reported by `dev.js _replayLoadMatch` through a new export `setReplayEventsScrubber(matchId, frames)` on every load (and `(null, 0)` on a failed load). After every render, and on every report, `_applySeekable` sets `disabled` on all listed rows when the ribbon's match is not the scrubber's ("These rows jump the scrubber once the match loads.") or has no frames ("No timeline frames are stored for this match, so these rows cannot jump the scrubber."), and shows that text in a new `<p id="replay-events-hint">` between the head and the list. When the match lands the SAME row nodes are enabled in place (no innerHTML rebuild). CSS: hover fill only on `:not(:disabled)`, `cursor: default` on `:disabled`; the row's `color: inherit` keeps the label colours (author rule beats the UA disabled grey); the hint is `--fs-sm` in `--text-dim`, `[hidden]` collapses.
- Alternatives: (a) `aria-disabled` and keep the rows focusable - rejected, the enable step would leave focus on a row that then changes role silently, and a disabled-looking tab stop that does nothing is the defect again for keyboard users; (b) hold B's rows until B's match lands - rejected in U-187 (couples two routes' render order, hides right data); (c) rebuild the rows on the match reply - rejected, a rebuild throws a focused row's focus to `<body>` (lane doc trap 2); (d) dim the disabled rows with opacity - rejected, it drops the kind-coloured labels below AA on `--surface-2` and the hint already says why; (e) queue the early click - rejected in U-187.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_replay_ribbon_seek_ready.py`, 4 tests (the fourth below): B's events in, match held (all 3 rows disabled, none tabbable, cursor default, hint text, hint between head and list, hint >= 4.5:1 on the section's canvas-resolved fill; after B loads the same marked nodes are enabled, cursor pointer, no hint, the 7:52 row seeks frame 2); B's match with `snapshots: []` (rows disabled, the no-frames hint); control: A loaded has live tabbable rows and an empty hidden hint. Red evidence: with HEAD's `replay_events.js` and `dev.js` only (the new markup and CSS kept), 2 failed on behaviour (`disabled 0 == 3`, cursor `pointer`, no hint) and the control passed; with all four HEAD web files the 3 failures are a TypeError on the missing hint element, which proves nothing on its own. A fourth test, `test_a_pick_that_empties_the_ribbon_drops_the_hint` (B pending with its hint up, then MOCK_ARAM_NEUTRAL, 0 events, its match held: no rows, no hint), was added for the verifier's MUST-FIX: `_renderEvents` returned from its empty branch before `_applySeekable`, so an emptied ribbon kept the hint; it now runs on both branches. Red with only that `return` restored: 1 failed. `test_replay_pending_pick_ribbon.py`: its pre-load click on B's disabled row is now `force=True` (Playwright never clicks a disabled button otherwise); its assertions are unchanged. Live-half digest rows re-captured: js_panels.txt (dev.js, replay_events.js), css_panels.txt (replay_events.css), web_other.txt (index.html) - code and markup changed, no glyph.
- 5-phase audit (independent verifier, headless, B's events in and B's match held, 1920x920 / 720x900 / 320x640): STRUCTURE - scrollWidth equals the viewport at all three; the hint sits 15 px inside the section each side, 8 px under the head and 8 px over the list, 22 / 22 / 65 px tall (three lines at 320); hidden it is `display: none`, height 0. TYPOGRAPHY - hint 18 px (`--fs-sm`). HIT-TARGETS - the disabled rows take no focus; after load the rows are 42 / 42 / 67 px (`--hit-min` 42) and Tab from the wards checkbox lands on the first row with `:focus-visible` and the inset ring. ASCII - 0 non-ASCII bytes in added lines, 0 CR in touched files (index.html's 51 non-ASCII bytes are on untouched lines). HIERARCHY - hint rgb(177,164,194) on the section background oklch(0.22 0.05 300) = rgb(30,20,46) is 7.50:1 (AA); disabled and enabled rows compute the same text, label and clock colours at opacity 1 (not greyed).
- FUTURE: the scrubber, clock and grid still show A under "loading B..." until B's match lands (the U-183 design; a matching "loading" state for the grid is unscoped).

### S144-U-189 - a Replay Timeline row that cannot seek no longer offers "Jump the scrubber to" on hover (s144 uiux cycle 179)

- Defect (the U-188 NEXT CANDIDATE nit): since U-188 a row of a match the scrubber does not hold yet, or of a match stored with no timeline frames, is a disabled button and the hint above the list says why, but its `title` - the native hover tooltip and the accessible description a screen reader reads with the row - still read "Jump the scrubber to 1:28", the one thing the row could not do. The flag row did the same ("You flagged this moment in game - jump the scrubber to 3:20").
- Ruling: `replay_events.js _applySeekable` keeps each row's own seek title on the node (`data-seek-title`, read from the markup on the first pass after a rebuild, since a rebuild makes fresh nodes) and sets `title` from `_blockedRowTitle` while the row is disabled: "Jumps the scrubber to <clock> once the match loads" (pending) or "No timeline frames are stored for this match, so this row cannot jump the scrubber to <clock>" (no frames). When the row can seek, the stored title goes back on the SAME node, so event rows read "Jump the scrubber to <clock>" again and the flag row its own longer title.
- Alternatives: (a) drop the title on a disabled row - rejected, the hint is above the list and may be scrolled away, so the row itself should still say why; (b) copy the list hint verbatim into every row ("These rows ...") - rejected, a per-row description that names "these rows" reads wrong one row at a time and names no clock; (c) emit both titles in the markup - rejected, two render paths (event and flag rows) would both need it, while one setter in `_applySeekable` covers every row that path disables.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_replay_ribbon_disabled_row_title.py` (3 tests; live /api path, B's events reply carries one synthetic flag pin at 200 s). B pending: all 4 rows disabled, each title is the pending text with its own clock, none contains "Jump the scrubber to"; B loads: the same marked nodes, enabled, each back on its own seek title (flag row included). B with `snapshots: []`: all 4 rows carry the no-frames text. Control: A loaded keeps "Jump the scrubber to <clock>". Red evidence: HEAD's `replay_events.js` -> 2 failed (`'Jump the scrubber to 1:28'` on a disabled row), control passed. Live-half digest row re-captured: js_panels.txt (replay_events.js; code, no glyph).
- 5-phase audit (independent verifier): STRUCTURE - no markup or DOM delta, the same row nodes keep their place, only `title` and `data-seek-title` change. TYPOGRAPHY - no CSS touched. HIT-TARGETS - no size, layout or focus change; `disabled` behaves as in U-188. ASCII - 0 non-ASCII and 0 CR bytes in the four touched files. HIERARCHY - no colour or visual change; only the tooltip and accessible-description text, each naming its own row's clock.
- FUTURE: how a native `title` tooltip shows on a disabled `<button>` varies by engine; the accessible description is correct either way.

### S144-U-190 - the Post Game Review team rosters and the LANE MATCHUP card fit every window width, live and archive (s144 uiux cycle 180)

- Row: the U-164 FUTURE "archive PGR body at 320 (417 in 282)". Re-measured with a populated archive match (headless Chromium, ui_mock `last_match_<mode>.json` served for `/api/last-match?match_ts=`, opened from a History row): the archive view scrolled sideways 853 in 602 at 640 down to 853 in 282 at 320 in SR / ARAM / Arena / Brawl (TFT has no roster). Sibling, same cause: the live view's Build tab (`#lm-tc-table`), same numbers (863 in Arena). Cause: `.lm-tc-row` is a fixed 8-track grid (9 with augments) plus a 200 px item strip = 778 px, so each ALLY / ENEMY group held 800 px whatever room it had: past the view from about 880 px down, and with the halves side by side from 1201 to about 1720 (1667 in 1163 at 1201; 1667 in 1662 at 1700). With augment icons loaded (Arena, 4 icons) the one-line augment strip made a row 874 px, 15 px wider than each 859 px 1920 half, so even the 1920 baseline scrolled sideways. Second sibling in the same tab: the LANE MATCHUP card (`pgr_lane_compare`) held both champion sides on one line and 296 px per metric row, past the view from about 500 px down (462 in 442 at 480, 382 in 282 at 320).
- Ruling (CSS only, `web/css/panels/last_match.css`, `web/css/panels/pgr_lane_compare.css`): the roster table's halves are `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(min(100%, 800px), 1fr))`, so ALLY | ENEMY sit side by side only while two whole groups fit (1920 keeps two halves) and stack otherwise. Each `.lm-tc-group` is an inline-size container; under 778 px (808 in augment mode) a row becomes a wrapping flex row: portrait, name and score, then level, KDA, summoners, augments, CS and items, each at its desktop track width, so those cells keep one left edge down the roster. Under 212 px the score leaves the name its line (name 32 -> 96 px at 320). The augment strip wraps inside its own auto track, so augment mode needs one icon column (808 px), not one per augment, and the 1920 Arena row stays the one-line grid. LANE MATCHUP: the sides wrap onto their own lines, and the card is an inline-size container; under 296 px a metric row puts label and delta on one line and the value body at full width under them.
- Alternatives: (a) one media breakpoint for the rows - rejected, the room a group gets depends on the 2-up / stacked state and the tab chrome, not the window, so a viewport threshold is wrong in the 1201-1720 band; (b) stack the halves at 1920 in augment mode - rejected, it moves the baseline when the augment strip can wrap in place; (c) shrink the 144 px name track in augment mode - rejected, it fits 4 icons by 0 px and fails at 5 or 6; (d) cut the nested lm-body / tabbed-section padding at 320 - out of scope, logged below.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_pgr_roster_reflow.py` (live sr / aram / arena / brawl on the Build tab, with a stubbed augment dictionary so Arena draws its icons; archive sr / arena; 11 widths 1920 to 320): no page error, no sideways scroll, every roster part inside the table and every cell inside its row, cells never overlap and keep reading order, name >= 40 px, 2-up only while two 800 px groups fit, one-line grid at full width, level / KDA / CS one left offset per roster, every LANE MATCHUP part inside its card. Red on HEAD's CSS: 57 failed, 23 passed. `tests/snapshot_panels/test_companion_reflow.py` read the first `.lm-row-half`, the roster table HIDDEN on the Build tab, so it measured a specified value nothing drew (and the new `repeat()` value split into 4 tokens); it now opens the Build tab with `&mode=sr` and reads `#lm-tc-table` rendered. Live-half digest row re-captured: css_panels.txt (last_match.css, pgr_lane_compare.css; code, no glyph).
- 5-phase audit (headless, 1920x920 / 1000 / 480 / 320): STRUCTURE - no sideways scroll at any width in any case; the 1920 and 1000 rows are the unchanged grid (59 px tall), wrapped rows 123 px at 480 and 219 px (SR) / 246 px (Arena) at 320. TYPOGRAPHY - no font declaration touched; computed name / KDA / level / CS / score 18 px, score badge 14 px (the existing operator exception), lane labels and deltas 16 px, at 1920 and 320 alike. HIT-TARGETS - no clickable added or resized; `.plc-side` keeps its 42 px min-height. ASCII - 0 non-ASCII, 0 CR in the five touched files. ### S144-U-192 - a Replay pick still loading no longer shows the previous match in the scrubber, clock, minimap and grid (s144 uiux cycle 182)

- Ruling: a pick puts the main pane in a pending state until its match reply lands. Closes the U-187 FUTURE row "the scrubber, clock and grid still show A under 'loading B...' until B's match lands". Before: `_replayLoadMatch` (`web/js/panels/dev.js`) rewrote only the meta line, so under "loading B..." the scrubber stayed live on A's frames (a drag moved A's clock and grid), A's clock, A's drawn minimap and A's 10 grid rows stayed on screen under the highlighted row B. Now (`_replayShowPending`): the scrubber is disabled at 0, the clock reads "-", the grid holds one "loading this match..." note row (the U-185 note box) and its table is `aria-busy`, the minimap canvas is cleared with its clock "-" and no flags, and the ribbon is told no match is in the scrubber. The reply clears `aria-busy` on success and on failure.
- The map figure is NOT hidden while pending: a shown figure keeps its own height with a blank canvas, so the map row and the grid under it do not collapse and re-grow on every pick; the reply then shows or hides it as before (hidden for queue 2400 and position-less matches). The pending call runs after `loadReplayEvents`, so the ribbon measures the height it holds while loading before the pending hint can add a line (measured: calling it first grew the held section 409 -> 439 px).
- Alternatives: (a) keep A's content but dim it - rejected, it still shows A's numbers under B's name and a dimmed slider is still draggable; (b) hide the map figure too, as the failed-load state does - rejected, the row collapses and re-grows on every successful pick; (c) skip the pending state when the same match is re-picked - rejected, a re-pick is a reload and the retry path for a failed pick.
- U-187 contract updated: `test_a_row_of_a_pending_pick_never_seeks_the_previous_match` now asserts B's early row leaves the PENDING scrubber, clock and grid unchanged and that A's clock is no longer shown, since A's frame is no longer on screen while B loads.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_replay_pending_pick_main_pane.py` (live /api path; A's reply carries synthetic in-bounds SR positions and per-frame timestamp_ms because the ui_mock rows store none, so A's minimap is drawn): with B's match reply held, scrubber disabled at 0, clock "-", grid exactly ["loading this match..."], table aria-busy, map figure shown at A's height, map clock "-", no flags, 0 inked canvas pixels; a dispatched scrubber input changes none of that; once B lands, enabled scrubber max 2, "t = 0.0min", B's 2 rows, no aria-busy. Red on HEAD's dev.js (the scrubber stayed live at 2 under "loading B...").
- 5-phase audit (headless, pending state, 1920x920 / 640x307 / 320x900): STRUCTURE - the note cell stays a 7-column table cell inside the grid pane (420-1844 in 420-1844; 66-574; 66-254), no sideways scroll; TYPOGRAPHY - the existing .replay-grid-note (18 px / 400 / muted), no new font rule; HIT-TARGETS - no new control; the disabled slider takes the existing disabled style (opacity 0.3, default cursor); focus stays on the picked row; ASCII - 0 non-ASCII, 0 CR in the touched files; HIERARCHY - meta line names the pick first, the grid note says the pick is loading. No MUST-FIX.

### S144-U-193 - the Replay ui_mock fixture draws the minimap, and the map note no longer moves the Timeline on a webfont swap where they stack (s144 uiux cycle 183)

- Ruling (fixture): closes the U-192 FUTURE row. `web/data/ui_mock/replay.json` now carries the shape `core/replay_history.py` `match_detail` serves: every snapshot of every match has its frame's own `timestamp_ms` (a few hundred ms past the minute; `minute = round(timestamp_ms / 60000, 1)`), and MOCK_NORMAL_DRAFT_WIN has a `pos` on all 40 frame entries and a `kills` list, one row per CHAMPION_KILL ribbon event (same second, killer, victim) with `pos` and `assists`. Values are REAL, copied read-only from one stored queue-400 match in `data/rewind_history.db`, seat by seat: frame times 0 / 600244 / 1200401 / 1800532, the 40 frame positions, and each kill's position and assists from the same killer and victim pair (4 of 5), else the same killer nearest in time (the 24:48 kill). Ids, names and stat lines stay synthetic (RM-906: no real match id, puuid or Riot ID). MOCK_RANKED_SOLO_WIN_ZERO_KDA keeps no `pos`, so the no-positions map state stays covered under ui_mock; Arena (no map queue) and ARAM neutral gain only `timestamp_ms`.
- Defect the refreshed fixture surfaced: with the SR row's map now drawn, `test_replay_font_swap.py` and `test_replay_filter_hit_targets.py` failed at 923x1280 (labels moved 18.19 px against RM-1072's 1 px). Cause, measured headless: at that width the Timeline wraps under the 300 px map figure, and the 76-character map note wraps to 2 lines in the first-paint fallback face but 3 in the Lato stub and in monospace, so the face swap moved the whole ribbon by one line. The same move happens on the live dashboard, whose matches always carry positions; the fixture had hidden it. Fix: `.replay-map-note { min-height: 3lh; }` (`web/css/panels/primitives.css`): the note is 57.56 px in every face (3 x 19.2 at 1/64 px rounding), labels move at most 1 px (the documented meta-row baseline px). 3 lines is the worst case at 300 px even in monospace; a narrower figure may still grow it (320 window: 4 lines, 76.8 px), min-height never clips.
- Alternatives: (a) put the Timeline before the map (flex `order`) - rejected, it also flips the 1920 baseline (map right of the ribbon), a design change for a stacked-only defect; (b) shorten the note to fit fewer lines - rejected, at 300 px and `--fs-xs` no honest wording fits 1-2 lines in every face; (c) lay the caption beside the canvas when stacked - rejected, 127 px left beside a 300 px canvas at 923.
- Guard: `tests/snapshot_panels/test_replay_mock_minimap.py`: fixture shape (timestamp_ms on every snapshot with its rounded minute; SR pos in the world square, team spawns within 1500 units of the fountains; kills one-to-one with the CHAMPION_KILL ribbon rows; ranked row has no pos) and the headless render at 1920x1080 (map shown and inked; "Map at 0:00 / 10:00 / 20:00 / 30:00" as the scrubber moves, 4 distinct canvas digests, no revive flag; a ribbon seek puts the map on the kill's own second, 3:50 and 24:48, while the grid shows the nearest frame; the note's computed min-height is 3 line-heights; the ranked row hides the map). Red on HEAD's fixture (map hidden, no timestamp_ms, no pos). The stacked move itself stays pinned by the two RM-1072 tests, which now exercise a shown map. Verifier mutation: removing the rule fails all three (18.19 px; min-height NaN).
- Guard data: `tests/guard_data/web_live_half/` re-captured for `primitives.css` (this rule) and `web/js/panels/dev.js`, whose row was left stale by cb6e5b983 (S144-U-192): the old pin equals the digest at cb6e5b983^.
- 5-phase audit (independent verifier, headless, 1920x920 / 923x1280 / 320x900): STRUCTURE - no sideways overflow at any size, the Timeline wraps under the figure at 923, the ranked row's figure is display none and 0x0; TYPOGRAPHY - note 16 px (`--fs-xs`), clock 18 px (`--fs-sm`), nothing under 16; HIT-TARGETS - no new control, the 3 filter labels 42 px at every size; ASCII - 0 non-ASCII, 0 CR in the touched files; HIERARCHY - in Lato the note fills its 3 lines, so no blank reserved line in the settled state; the 1920 row stays 409 px (section 409 > figure 391). No MUST-FIX.
- FUTURE: the ribbon's ui_mock CHAMPION_KILL events carry no `pos` (the real /api/replay/events rows do, `dashboard/routes_replay_events.py`); nothing in web/ reads it today. A ribbon seek uses `clock_s * 1000`, which lands before a kill's own ms (230000 < 230254), so at the seek moment the victim is not yet dead on the map - live behaviour too, not a fixture artefact.
